- The content fetches merge the read policies that differ only in `User_Subtree` and leave out those that cannot
  give access to a fetched node (`eZContentObjectTreeNode::mergeLimitationList()`, `pruneLimitationList()`,
  `permissionFetchScope()`, optional fourth parameter of `createPermissionCheckingSQL()`). The children of a folder
  in a teamroom took 24.4 ms with 800 read policies, now 1.2 ms; the fetches return the same nodes. New setting
  `site.ini [RoleSettings] PermissionSQLOptimization` (`enabled`; `disabled` ORs every policy as before). Guide:
  [Permission checks for users with many role assignments](../../features/6.0/permission-checks-for-many-assignments.md).

### Fixed

- On SQLite every content fetch of a user with 1,000 read policies or more failed ("Expression tree is too large")
  and listed nothing. Merged, the 1,203 policies of the member above are 5, with 240 subtree conditions, and OR
  chains of more than 100 parts are written in groups (`permissionSQLOr()`), so thousands of policies or subtrees
  stay below the limit. Tests: `eZContentPermissionSQLOptimizationTest` (no database),
  `eZContentPermissionSQLOptimizationLiveTest` (live).
- Before it reached main, the merging was corrected: the subtrees of a merged policy are put in parentheses (without
  them `Class AND subtree 1 OR subtree 2` gave subtree 2 to every class and listed nodes users may not read), a
  `Class` limitation without values no longer leaves its policy out under a class filter, class ids are compared as
  numbers and the class filter type as the fetches read it, and policies that add no condition, left alone after
  the others were left out, give `0 = 1` rather than no condition. Checked on the demonstration server for every user, the read
  policies of every role and throwaway users of every limitation kind: 3,294 fetches, 28,224 access checks and 2,712
  create lists give the same answers as before.

## 6 October 2026: renamed nodes keep their old addresses as redirects

### Fixed

- On MySQL, PostgreSQL, SQLite and Oracle, renaming or moving a node left its old address as a second original
  address instead of a history entry: the old link kept answering 200 instead of 301 to the new address, and a node
  could have several original addresses per language. `eZURLAliasML::storePath()` had lost its SQL history step
  when the MongoDB support was added; it runs again for every driver except MongoDB (an old entry of the language
  becomes history and links to the new one, an entry shared with other languages only loses the language's bit,
  children are reparented, custom and global aliases are not touched). On an installation that ran without it,
  publishing a node again, or `updateSubTreePath()`, turns its stale addresses into redirects; run such scripts
  with the siteaccess the editors publish from (`-s admin`), since a siteaccess with another `RootNode` names the
  content root differently. Tests: `eZURLAliasMLHistoryLiveTest` (live), `eZURLAliasMLMongoStorePathTest`.
- On MongoDB, a URL alias entry was stored without the always-available bit of its object, and every call of
  `storePath()` warned about an undefined `$alwaysMask`.

## 6 October 2026: no read access lists no content, and a disabled user is signed out once

### Fixed

- A user without any `content/read` policy, and a user whose access array could not be read (fail-closed), was
  listed every node by the content fetches, search, trash, keyword lists and the admin's child counts:
  `createPermissionCheckingSQL( false )` gave no condition. It now gives `0 = 1`; full access still gives none. See
  [the behaviour changes of October 2026](../../bc/6.0/behaviour-changes-2026-10.md). Test:
  `eZContentPermissionSQLTest::testNoReadAccessGivesAConditionNoNodeMeets`.
- A disabled user in the session ended the request in "Allowed memory size exhausted": `eZUser::currentUser()` signed
  the user out, the audit record of the sign-out asked `currentUser()` who acts, which signed out again, without end.
  While the sign-out is recorded, `currentUser()` now answers with the user being signed out (`eZUser::$recordingLogout`,
  reset in a `finally` block and by Velocity between requests). Test: `eZUserDisabledCurrentUserLiveTest` (live).

## 7 October 2026: logs, INI cache and expiry timestamps per site

### Added

- Settings for sites with a VarDir of their own on one installation (multi-site hosting), all in `site.ini
  [FileSettings]` and off by default: `UseGlobalLogDir=disabled` writes the debug logs and the default logs of
  `eZLog` into the log directory of the site, `LogDir` may be an absolute path, `ExpiryDir` keeps `expiry.php`
  outside the cache directory (for a cache on a memory file system), `INICacheDir=site` caches the INI files of a site
  in its own cache directory, `LogVarDir` and `CacheVarDir` (e.g. `var_log`, `var_cache`) put the logs or caches of
  every site into a tree of their own with one line (`var/<site>` → `var_cache/<site>`); Velocity serves the public
  caches there (`expVelocity::staticPaths()`), `.htaccess_root` and the nginx example have the rule to enable. `eZSiteAccess::change()` derives all of them again on every change, for web requests,
  Velocity workers and scripts. New: `eZSys::logDirectory()`, `eZSys::relocatedVarDirectory()`, `eZDebug::setLogDirectory()` and `logDirectory()`,
  `eZExpiryHandler::filePath()`, `eZSiteAccess::updateINICacheDirectory()` and `updateLogDirectory()`,
  `eZUpdateDebugLogDirectory()`, `eZSys::cacheDirectoryOf()` (the cache directory of another siteaccess, which the
  classic menu, the toolbar and the package datatype now use instead of a path of their own). Guide:
  [Logs, INI cache and expiry timestamps per site](../../features/6.0/multi-site-log-and-cache-paths.md). Tests:
  `eZSiteLogAndExpiryPathsTest`, `eZSiteAccessSitePathsTest`, `eZSiteLogReadersTest` (no database), cases in
  `expVelocityEnginesTest`.

### Updated

- What reads the logs follows where they are written: the setup log counts the errors of a step in the `error.log`
  eZDebug writes (the log directory of the site with `UseGlobalLogDir=disabled`), and Setup > System information names
  that directory, warns when it cannot be written and finds the cronjob logs there. Audit records stay inside VarDir
  (`audit.ini [AuditSettings] LogDir`), which `LogVarDir` does not move; the old text files of 4.x audit names now
  honour an absolute `[AuditSettings] LogDir`, and an absolute VarDir is no longer put inside the installation.
- Velocity serves the public caches below a `CacheVarDir` set for some siteaccesses only (read from every siteaccess
  when it starts), and its warm-up puts the log and INI cache directories of the site it rendered back
  (`eZSiteAccess::resetSitePaths()`).
- `eZDir::recursiveDelete()` deletes inside the cache, log, expiry and INI cache directories of the site also when
  site.ini puts them outside the root, and the cache directory as a whole, as it does inside the root. A directory
  that contains the installation is never one of them. An `[FileSettings] AllowedDeletionDirs` entry that does not exist allows
  nothing (it allowed every path), and an entry is a directory, not a prefix: `/srv/cache` no longer allows
  `/srv/cache-other`. An installation that relied on either lists the directory it deletes from.
- Velocity reads the `CacheVarDir` of every siteaccess on an `eZINI` of its own when it starts, without
  `eZSiteAccess::load()`: no extension is activated, no INI or extension cache file is written and the current
  siteaccess, the exception handler and the global INI instances stay as they were.
- The Velocity warm-up finds the installation root and the snapshot of the globals its entry script takes, which it
  lost when its code moved into `VelocityWarmup::run()` (the pool requires the entry script inside a function): a site
  in maintenance is no longer rendered (the marker was looked for at `/var/maintenance.json`), each render has
  `SCRIPT_FILENAME` `<root>/index.php`, and the final sweep removes only the globals added after the snapshot instead
  of every global but the superglobals and `Q.webserver.keepGlobals`. Without a snapshot the sweep is skipped.

## 7 October 2026: the dialogs of the online editor and pasted styles

### Fixed

- The dialogs of the online editor (`ezoe/dialog`, `ezoe/relations`) open for whoever may read the object, as before,
  and for whoever edits the version named in the address: the editors of a draft of an object that was never
  published, which no one can read yet, could not insert a link, an image or an embedded object. "Edits the version"
  is decided as `content/edit` decides it: the version is a draft (a draft, an internal draft or one to be repeated)
  of that object made by the current user, the object is not in the trash, and `eZContentObject::editAccess()` allows
  it in the version's own language (a Language limitation applies, a listener of `content/edit/access` has its say).
  Someone else's draft, a published, pending or archived version, or a version that does not exist opens nothing for
  who may not read the object. `ezoe/dialog` opens only a dialog whose name is a plain template name (letters,
  digits, `_` and `-`). `Exponential\View\Extension\Ezoe\Ezoe\Dialog::mayOpen()`, `::mayEditVersion()`,
  `::isDialogName()`. Tests: `expOEDialogAccessRulesTest` (no database), `expOEDialogAccessTest` (live).
- A pasted style declaration without a colon (`style="color; text-align: right"`) raised two PHP warnings; it is left
  out now, and a value keeps the colons it holds (an address). Only a plain CSS property name becomes an attribute: a
  declaration named `xmlns` or `xml...` no longer puts a namespace declaration into the stored XML (with `xmlns:x` the
  stored XML did not load again without a warning). An alignment (`text-align`, `float`, `align`) is taken only as a
  keyword, in lower case, without `!important` and without a browser prefix (`-webkit-center` is `center`); markup, a
  script address or an expression in it is left out.
  Tests: `expOEPastedStyleRulesTest` (no database), `expOEPastedStylesTest` (live).

## 7 October 2026: uploads, the embed dialog and the markup of the online editor

### Fixed

- An upload from the online editor (`ezoe/upload`) asked only for edit access to the object, whatever version number
  the address named, so it could create an object and relate it to someone else's draft or to a published or
  archived version. It now writes only into a draft of the user's own that they may edit in its language, decided as
  `content/edit` decides it (`Dialog::mayEditVersion()`), and checks that before it fetches or creates anything.
  `ezoe/upload` (the dialog) and `ezoe/tags` open by the rule of the other dialogs together with edit access to the
  object: whoever could open them before still can, and who may edit but not read the object opens them only for a
  draft of their own. `Dialog::mayOpenForEditing()`, `Upload::mayUpload()`.
- The uploaded object became a relation of version 1 (the version of the new object) instead of the version being
  edited; it now goes to the version being edited. `Upload::addEmbedRelation()`.
- The online editor put the stored class, align, width, border, colspan, rowspan, href, target, title, id, view, size
  and anchor name, the name of a custom tag and of an embedded object, the address of a custom tag image and the style
  of a custom attribute into its markup unescaped, so a stored value with a quote could add attributes or markup in
  the editor. Each value is escaped once where it goes into the markup (`eZOEXMLInput::markupValue()`); values that are
  escaped already are not escaped again, and opening and saving gives the stored values back unchanged. The text of a
  header with an anchor is escaped as other text is.
- `ezoe/relations` without an EmbedID, or with a malformed one, raised undefined variable and undefined key warnings
  before its message; it ends with the message now. The EmbedID is an object id, `eZObject_<id>` or `eZNode_<id>`
  (`Relations::parseEmbedId()`).
- The editor no longer raises deprecation notices for a missing user agent on the command line, no longer rebuilds the
  style map of the custom attributes on every call when `ezoe.ini` gives none, and saving a paragraph whose custom tag
  was removed no longer reads a missing parent.
  Tests: `expOEDialogAccessRulesTest`, `expOEMarkupEscapeTest`, `expOERelationsEmbedIdTest` (no database),
  `expOEUrlFetcherTest`, `expOEEditorViewAccessTest` (live).
- `ezoe/load` reads its EmbedID as `ezoe/relations` does (`Relations::parseEmbedId()`): a missing or malformed one
  answers `false` without a PHP warning. The content type of `ezoe/upload` and `ezoe/relations`, which goes into the
  path of a template (`design:ezoe/upload_<type>.tpl`, `design:ezoe/tag_embed_<type>.tpl`), must be one of the
  editor's types (objects, images, files) or a relation group of `content.ini [RelationGroupSettings]`, and a plain
  template name; anything else ends with a message. `Dialog::contentTypes()`, `Dialog::isContentType()`.
  Test: `expOEContentTypeAndLoadTest` (no database).

## 7 October 2026: copy the debug bar's messages

### Added

- A **Copy Messages** button on the Messages tab of the debug bar copies the messages that are shown (level buttons
  and filter box applied) as plain text: "Level: source", a tab and the time, then the message, a blank line between
  entries. It copies with `navigator.clipboard` and falls back to a hidden text field where the page is not a secure
  context; a status line says "Copied N messages" or that the copy failed. Translated in eng-US and ger-DE (context
  `design/standard/debugbar`). `expDebugBarReport::copyMessagesHtml()`. Guide:
  [The Exp Debug bar](../../features/6.0/exp-debug-bar.md). Test: `expDebugBarReportCopyMessagesTest` (no database).

## 7 October 2026: downloads from streams that return short reads

### Fixed

- `eZFile::downloadContent()` added the size it asked `fread()` for to the bytes sent, not the size it got. A stream
  that returns less per read (a userland stream wrapper hands back one 8 KB buffer, network and cluster mounts return
  what has arrived) ended the transfer after the first read, and the client got a truncated file with status 200.
  It now counts what was read and stops at the end of the file or an empty read; ranges and plain files behave as
  before. The DFS/MySQLi cluster gateway (`index_cluster.php`) sent a range with a single `fread()` and had the same
  result; it reads until the requested length. Test: `eZFileDownloadShortReadTest` (no database).

## 7 October 2026: content limitations of extensions in searches

### Added

- A content limitation handler can also restrict searches in a search engine that filters by the policies of the
  user itself, such as eZ Find: it implements `ezpContentLimitationSolrHandler` (`solrFilter()`) besides
  `ezpContentLimitationHandler`. The search extension asks `ezpContentLimitation::solrFilter()`, which gives the
  filter in parentheses, to join with AND to the other limitations of the policy. When the policy gives no access in
  the search the filter is `ezpContentLimitation::DENY_SOLR`, which matches no document, and never `false`: a search
  extension that left the policy out would filter by nothing once no policy is left. That happens without a handler,
  for a handler without the interface, for a kernel limitation, when the handler fails, and for an answer that is
  not a self-contained filter (string filters may not leave their parentheses or range brackets, nor use local
  parameters or nested queries). Field conditions (`array( 'field' => ..., 'values' => ..., 'not' => false )`) are
  written and escaped by the kernel (`ezpContentLimitation::solrValue()`, which also keeps a value `AND`, `OR` or
  `NOT` a term); a `not` that is not a boolean, or a value that is not UTF-8, gives no access.
  Guide: [Content policy limitations of extensions](../../features/6.0/content-limitation-handlers.md#searches).
  Test: `ezpContentLimitationSolrTest`, including an allow/deny matrix of mixed policies.

## 8 October 2026: the session cookie in every response, and the flags of is_logged_in

### Added

- `site.ini [Session] CookieAlwaysAddToHttpResponse` (`disabled` by default): with it enabled, the session cookie is
  sent with every response of a running session, not only when the session starts, for a load balancer that keeps a
  user on one server by the session id in the cookie. Same name, value, path, domain, lifetime and flags as the
  session cookie (a lifetime counts again from each response), sent once (not again when PHP already set it in the
  response), and nothing without a running session: an anonymous visitor without a session gets none. Such a
  response is kept out of shared caches: its `Cache-Control` (all its lines, read together) becomes `private`
  (`public`, `s-maxage` and `proxy-revalidate` dropped, `max-age` and the rest kept for the browser; `no-store` needs
  no `private`), a `Surrogate-Control` becomes `no-store` and an `X-Accel-Expires` `0`, so no proxy, CDN or service
  worker stores a session id for the next visitor. Velocity's response cache stores no response that sets a cookie anyway; the role-aware HTTP cache
  (`httpcache.ini`) keeps pages without their cookies, and a page it answers before the kernel starts carries no
  session cookie. Off, nothing changes. `eZSession::sendResponseCookie()`, `responseCookie()`,
  `cookieOnEveryResponse()`, `sessionCookie()`, `privateCacheControl()`, `privateCacheHeaders()`, `listsCookie()`.

### Updated

- The `is_logged_in` cookie (for HTTP caches) is sent with the path, domain, lifetime, `Secure` and `SameSite` of the
  session cookie (`site.ini [Session] CookiePath`, `CookieDomain`, `CookieTimeout`, `CookieSecure`, `CookieSameSite`),
  so it goes wherever and as long as the session cookie does; it was sent without flags, for the installation
  directory and until the browser closed. It stays readable by scripts (not `HttpOnly`), as scripts of cached pages
  read it. With `CookieAlwaysAddToHttpResponse` enabled it is sent with every response of a signed-in user, as the
  session cookie is. An `is_logged_in` set before this change for another path or without the domain is not removed
  at sign-out and stays until the browser closes; a cache then only passes that browser's requests to the server.
  `eZSession::siteCookieOptions()`; the parameters of the session cookie are available without setting them:
  `eZSession::cookieParams()`.
  Test: `eZSessionResponseCookieTest` (no database; several requests in one process as in a persistent worker).

## 8 October 2026: updates of persistent objects on Oracle

### Fixed

- `eZPersistentObject::storeObject()` wrote the WHERE condition of the UPDATE of an existing row with the long field
  names, while the field list, the INSERT and the lookup of the row use the short names of a database that has them
  (Oracle, `eZDBInterface::useShortNames()`). A key with a short name named a column the table does not have, and the
  update failed. In the kernel this is `eZEnumObjectValue` (`contentobject_attribute_version` is
  `contentobject_attr_version`), whose row is updated when a value is stored again for the same attribute version;
  `eZURLObjectLink` has the same key but no other fields, so it is never updated. The condition now uses the short
  names too.
- The other queries `eZPersistentObject` builds name a field with a short name by it as well: ORDER BY and GROUP BY of
  `fetchObjectList()`, the SET and WHERE of `updateObjectList()`, the order column and condition of
  `newObjectOrder()`, and the UPDATEs of `reorderObject()`. A long text field with a short name that is bound as a
  variable (more than 2000 characters on Oracle) was written into the UPDATE as a literal instead of its bind variable.
  A column with a short name that is NULL came back under its short name, and a definition whose fields are plain
  strings stopped with a TypeError on Oracle. No kernel definition hits these today; they matter for extension
  definitions with short names.
- `bin/php/convertezenumtoezselection.php` removed the converted enum values with its own SQL and the long column
  name; it removes them through `eZEnumObjectValue`'s definition now.
- Databases without short names (MySQL, PostgreSQL, SQLite, MongoDB) get exactly the same queries as before. Test:
  `eZPersistentObjectShortNameTest` (no database; compares every query with and without short names). Verified end to
  end against Oracle Database 23ai Free with the ezoracle driver: every path above works there; the UPDATE, ORDER BY,
  GROUP BY, `updateObjectList()` and `newObjectOrder()` failed there before.

## 8 October 2026: notifications for some classes only, and notify for eZContentFunctions

### Added

- `notification.ini [NotificationSettings] NotificationFilterByClassIdentifier` and `IncludeClasses[]`: with the
  setting enabled, only publications of the listed classes make a notification event; any other class counts as
  published without notification (the filter `content/notification/create` gets `false`). `disabled` by default,
  so nothing changes without it. The list takes class identifiers; an entry of digits is taken as a class ID.
  `eZContentOperationCollection::notificationIncludesClass()`.
- `eZContentFunctions::createAndPublishObject( $params, $notify = true )` and `updateAndPublishObject( $object,
  $params, $notify = true )` pass `notify` to the publish operation, so imports and scripts can publish without
  notification. Callers without the argument notify as before; `false`, `0` and `"0"` mean no, anything else yes
  (`eZContentOperationCollection::notifyRequested()`, which the publish operation's notification step uses too).
  Guide: [Publish without notification](../../features/6.0/publish-without-notification.md#notifications-for-some-classes-only).
  Tests: `eZPublishWithoutNotificationTest` (PN-13 to PN-15, no database), `eZPublishWithoutNotificationLiveTest`
  (PL-05 to PL-09, live, with the file mail transport set in-process).

### Fixed

- `eZContentFunctions::createAndPublishObject()` returned the object it had instantiated, which still said draft
  (status, no current version published, no main node) after the publish operation had published it; it now returns
  the object as stored after publishing.

## 8 October 2026: limits for the modes of new files and directories

### Added

- The constants `EZP_DIR_MODE_MAX` and `EZP_FILE_MODE_MAX` (in `config.php`, see `config.php-RECOMMENDED`) set the
  widest mode any directory and any file the installation creates may get. A mode asked for is only narrowed (0777
  becomes 0750, 0600 stays 0600), and `autoload.php` sets the umask of the process from them, so files written
  without a mode of their own stay inside them as well. Without the constants nothing changes. `eZFile::fileMode()`,
  `eZDir::dirMode()`, `eZFile::creationUmask()` in place of `umask( 0 )`, `eZFile::applyCreationUmask()`; used by
  `eZDir::mkdir()`, `eZDir::directoryPermission()`, `eZFile::create()`, `eZLog`, `eZDebug`, `eZINI` (cache directory,
  cache and saved files), `eZPHPCreator`, `eZFileHandler`, the autoload generator, uploads (`eZHTTPFile`), image
  variations (`eZImageHandler`), the cluster file handlers and the static cache. One constant gives the other limit;
  a value that is no mode, or a limit that would take rights from the owner (`0`), is ignored (no limit, as without
  it) and reported once to the PHP error log. With 0770/0660 for a web server and command line scripts of different
  users, the directories of `var/` need the set-group-ID bit of the common group (see the guide).
  Guide: [Limits for the modes of new files and directories](../../bc/6.0/file-modes.md).
  Test: `expFileModeLimitsTest` (security suite, no database), which also fails for a new native `chmod()`, `mkdir()`
  or `umask()` that bypasses the helpers; the calls still to be moved are listed in it.

### Fixed

- With the limits set, the autoload generator creates `var/autoload` with a directory mode instead of
  `EZP_INI_FILE_PERMISSION` (a file mode, which left the directory without its search bit) and limits its files by
  `EZP_FILE_MODE_MAX`, which takes away the execute bits of the 0777 they got. Without the limits both stay as they
  were.

## 8 October 2026: every new file and directory within the limits

### Updated

- Every native `chmod()`, `mkdir()` and `umask()` in the kernel, the libraries, the scripts and the extensions of the
  repository now goes through `eZFile::fileMode()`, `eZDir::dirMode()`, the new `eZFile::executableMode()` (for the
  FrankenPHP binary and the scripts `bin/php/console` runs, limited by `EZP_DIR_MODE_MAX` so they stay executable)
  or `eZFile::creationUmask()`: the audit trail, content jobs, Velocity and FrankenPHP, the INI editor, mail
  preferences, setup and installer, HTTP cache, debug bar, maintenance, notifications, packages, templates written in
  the admin, shop receipts, the query cache and others; command line scripts without the `.php` ending are checked
  too. Without `EZP_DIR_MODE_MAX` / `EZP_FILE_MODE_MAX` the modes are the same as before;
  narrow umasks set on purpose stay at least as narrow. `expAuditWriter::ownLikeParent()` limits a directory as a
  directory and a file as a file. The list of calls still to be moved in `expFileModeLimitsTest` is empty, so the test
  fails for any new call that bypasses the helpers. The HTTP cache early exit and the repair queue, which run before
  the autoloader, load the helpers themselves. Files that the web server and the command line both write need one
  user, or one group with 0770/0660 and set-group-ID directories, once limits are set. Files written without a
  mode of their own keep to both limits, also for a pair that does not match (0750/0600 gives 0600 and 0710). Guide:
  [Limits for the modes of new files and directories](../../bc/6.0/file-modes.md).

## 8 October 2026: the column conditions of content limitation handlers

### Fixed

- A column condition that a content limitation handler returns from `permissionSQL()`
  (`array( 'column' => ..., 'values' => ..., 'not' => ... )`) was inverted by any `not` that PHP reads as true, so
  `'not' => 'false'` turned "only these" into "all but these", and with no values into a condition that matches
  every row; `'not' => 0` did the reverse. As in search filters, only a real boolean inverts now: any other `not`
  makes the answer no condition, and the policy gives no access in fetches (`ezpContentLimitation::DENY_SQL`).
  `ezpContentLimitation::sqlCondition()` and `solrCondition()` share the check.
  Guide: [Content policy limitations of extensions](../../features/6.0/content-limitation-handlers.md).
  Test: `ezpContentLimitationTest`.

## 8 October 2026: fatal errors of the kernel without E_USER_ERROR

### Fixed

- PHP 8.4 deprecates passing `E_USER_ERROR` to `trigger_error()`. The kernel did so when a MySQLi query failed
  with the standard error handling, when the cluster gateway (`index_cluster.php`, `ezpClusterGateway`) answered
  with a 500, when `eZExpiryHandler` could not read `expiry.php`, and in `eZDebug::writeError()` while errors are
  handed to PHP (a command line script before `eZScript::initialize()`, or a message written while a log file is
  written). They call the new `eZFatalUserError::raise()`, which does what PHP did, without the deprecation: the
  error handler that is set gets `E_USER_ERROR` with the message, file and line, and the script goes on when the
  handler takes it (eZDebug's handler, set for every web request, logs the error and returns, as before); without
  a handler, or when it answers false, the script stops with an uncaught `eZFatalUserError` (an `Error`, so code
  that catches `Exception` does not take it): exit code 255 on the command line, a 500 on the web, and a
  persistent worker (Velocity) answers the request with a 500 and serves the next one instead of ending.
  Test: `eZFatalUserErrorTest`, which also fails for any new call that passes `E_USER_ERROR` to `trigger_error()`.

## 9 October 2026: SOAP calls to servers with an internal certificate

### Added

- `eZSOAPClient::setCAFile()` trusts the CA certificates of a file of its own for the certificate of an HTTPS server,
  for a service whose certificate an internal CA issued. `setVerifyPeer( false )` turns the check off for one client
  only and logs a warning on every call; by default the certificate is verified, as before. `caFile()` and
  `verifyPeer()` read them; `curlOptions()` gives the cURL options of a call. A class extending `eZSOAPClient` that
  declares members of these names has to rename them.
  Guide: [SOAP calls over HTTPS](../../features/6.0/soap-client-tls.md). Test: `eZSOAPClientTlsOptionsTest` (no server).

### Fixed

- `eZSOAPClient` without the PHP extension curl sent a call over HTTPS as plain text to the TLS port; it is refused
  with an error now (`send()` returns `0`, `ErrorString` and the error log say that HTTPS needs curl). This is a
  behaviour change: see [Behaviour changes of 1 to 2 October 2026](../../bc/6.0/behaviour-changes-2026-10.md),
  "Missing libraries". Test: `eZSOAPClientTlsOptionsTest` (in a PHP started without curl).
- `eZSOAPClient::setCAFile()`: a name with a NUL byte sets no CA file (reported), instead of a `ValueError` in
  `send()`; under `open_basedir` a CA file outside it is no longer reported as unreadable on every call, since cURL
  reads it anyway.

## 9 October 2026: leaving out a cronjob part

### Added

- `runcronjobs.php` asks the `ezpEvent` filter `cronjob/part/run` with the part, the siteaccess, the scripts and
  whether a single script runs (`--script`) before the first script of a part runs. A listener in `site.ini [RunnableSettings] Listeners[]` returns `true` to
  run the part; anything else leaves it out with a notice and exit code 0, so a stop switch for a release no longer
  needs a patched `runcronjobs.php`. A listener that throws ends the run with exit code 1 before any script runs.
  Without listeners every part runs as before. Also on **Setup > RAD**.
  [Feature](../../features/6.0/cronjob-part-filter.md). Test: `CronjobPartRunFilterTest` (no database).

## 9 October 2026: sendmail without an envelope sender

### Added

- `site.ini [MailSettings] SendmailEnvelopeSender=disabled` makes the sendmail transport leave out `-f <sender>`.
  msmtp with `--read-envelope-from` (for instance `sendmail_path="msmtp --read-envelope-from -t"`) refuses `-f`
  together with that option, so every mail failed there. A mail without a sender of its own (no sender address and
  no `From` extra header) then gets a From header with `EmailSender`, else `AdminEmail`, when one of them is a valid
  address (also written `Name <address>`); msmtp reads the envelope sender from it. The header is written for that
  one call: the `eZMail` object keeps the sender it had. The default `enabled` keeps `-f` and the options as before.
  Test: `eZSendmailEnvelopeSenderTest` (no database; `mail()` runs in a child process against a capturing
  `sendmail_path`, which the child checks before it sends, so nothing is sent).

## 9 October 2026: one User-Agent for the mails of the site

### Added

- `site.ini [MailSettings] UserAgent` sets the User-Agent header of every mail sent through `eZMail` (sendmail,
  SMTP and file transports; ASCII only), for instance the name of the portal. Empty (the default) keeps the mails
  as they were: `Exponential, Version <version>` over sendmail and file, `Apache Zeta Components` over SMTP.
  `eZMail::setUserAgent()` still sets it for one mail. Line breaks in the setting do not start a header.
  Guide: [After installing](../../install/10-after-installing.md), 10.4. Test: `eZMailUserAgentTest` (no database).

### Fixed

- `eZMail::setUserAgent()` reaches mails sent by SMTP. The mail library wrote its own User-Agent when it generated
  the headers, so the value was lost there; `ezpMail` now keeps the one eZMail gave it. Code that already calls
  `setUserAgent()` therefore sends its own name over SMTP from now on instead of `Apache Zeta Components`; over
  sendmail and file nothing changes. `ezpMail::$userAgent` is public: a value set on it directly gets the same line
  break cleaning as `setUserAgent()`, so it cannot add a header either.

## 9 October 2026: who trashed what, in the database

### Updated

- Who moved an object to the trash and from where are columns of the trash row now:
  `ezcontentobject_trash.trashed_by` (user content object id) and `trashed_via` (`web <siteaccess>`,
  `cli <script>`), written by `eZContentObjectTrashNode::createFromNode()` in the same transaction as the row.
  `<VarDir>/trash/trashed.json`, which was local to one web server and outside transactions and backups, is no
  longer written once the columns exist; the trash view still reads it for older rows, and
  `update/common/scripts/6.0/movetrashrecords.php` copies it into the columns. The filter by user runs in SQL. The
  database update of each engine adds the columns. [Guide](../../bc/6.0/trash.md#where-trashed-by-is-kept). Tests:
  `TrashedByColumnsTest` (no database), `eZContentObjectTrashRecordTest` (installation database).
- Code and database update can come in either order. Until the columns exist,
  `eZContentObjectTrashNode::hasTrashedByColumns()` says so (asked of the database's catalogue, never by a query
  that could fail inside a transaction, again on each trash move until the columns are found, so a Velocity worker
  needs no restart) and `definition()` leaves them out: moving content to the trash stores the row without them and
  records who did it in the file as before, and fetching a trash row, the trash view with its filter by user and
  restoring keep working. Before, every move to the trash ended with "A database transaction failed" until the
  update had run. Test: `eZContentObjectTrashOldSchemaTest` (an installation database without the columns; skipped
  on an updated one), `TrashedByColumnsTest`.
- `trashed_via` holds printable ASCII within 100 bytes (anything else becomes `?`): a script name in another
  encoding is no valid UTF-8, which PostgreSQL refuses inside the trash move's transaction, and 100 multibyte
  characters exceed a column that counts bytes (Oracle). Test: `TrashedByColumnsTest`.
- `movetrashrecords.php` stops with exit status 1 when `trashed.json` holds no JSON object (cut short, edited by
  hand): it was read as empty, so `--remove-file` deleted it with every entry. Its line of counts adds the rows whose
  entry names no user, so the numbers add up. An entry without `node_id` or `trashed` matches no row without a PHP
  warning. Tests: `TrashedByColumnsTest`, `eZContentObjectTrashRecordTest`.
- The trash view no longer reads the whole trash for every page: `TrashList` asks the database for what the page
  needs (the trash rows among the ancestors of its items, a count on the indexed `path_string` for the nodes below
  each, counts in SQL for the summary and the user list), and `ezcontentobject_trash` gets the index
  `ezcobj_trash_trashed_by` (in the same update files). With 100,000 rows in the trash a page took 2.7–3.2 s and
  160 MB on SQLite, now 0.54–0.90 s and 2–3 MB; the measurements are in the
  [guide](../../bc/6.0/trash.md#a-large-trash). The user list and the "known for" count of the
  summary count trash rows, not objects. On MongoDB the summary and the user list come from aggregations (the driver
  translates neither `NOT EXISTS` nor `SUM( CASE ... )` nor `GROUP BY`, so they showed nothing); a path that ends in
  a line break is refused by `belowCondition()` like any other non-path. Tests: `TrashedByColumnsTest`,
  `TrashListPagedTest`, `eZContentObjectTrashRecordTest`.
- MongoDB keeps who trashed what in the trash documents too, and its trash view works. `hasTrashedByColumns()`
  asks the driver's schema (`expMongoDB::declaredColumns()`, from the shipped `.dba`), which declares both fields;
  new trash documents carry them. The trash list and its count were one SQL statement with joins, which the driver
  cannot translate: on MongoDB the view listed and counted nothing, and so emptied nothing. They now come from one
  aggregation (`eZContentObjectTrashNode::trashListMongo()`, `trashListFilterMongo()`, `trashListSortMongo()`) with
  the same filters, sort keys and paging; a user whose `content/read` is limited by policies is shown an empty
  trash rather than all of it, and an `AttributeFilter` likewise lists nothing. The class filter's options
  (`TrashList::classOptions()`, also a join) come from two single-table reads. Nothing changes on the SQL engines.
  [Guide](../../bc/6.0/trash.md#where-trashed-by-is-kept). Test: `TrashListMongoTest`.
- The MongoDB driver reads and compares a field that a document lacks as the SQL engines read the column: a column
  the `.dba` declares NOT NULL with a default holds that default in every row of a SQL table, also in a row written
  before the column was added, while a document written before then has no field, which MongoDB's `$eq`, `$in` and
  ranges never match and `$ne` and `$nin` always do. So "WHERE trashed_by = 0" missed every trash document older
  than the field, and `movetrashrecords.php` reported rows as given a `trashed_by` that its UPDATE never reached.
  The WHERE of UPDATE, DELETE and single-table SELECT now matches the missing field exactly when the default
  satisfies the comparison (=, <>, IN, NOT IN, <, <=, >, >=; IS NULL and LIKE are unchanged), and a SELECT that names
  such a column returns the default for a document without it, NULL for a declared column without one. Test:
  `expMongoDBDeclaredDefaultTest` (also against a real server with `EXP_TEST_MONGO_SCRATCH`).

## 9 October 2026: keeping items in the trash for a number of days

### Added

- `content.ini [TrashSettings] KeepItemsForDays` makes the `trashpurge` cronjob part purge only what has been in the
  trash for at least that many days. Empty or 0, the default, purges everything as before; a value that is not a
  whole number of days up to 36500 purges nothing and reports an error. `bin/php/trashpurge.php
  --trashed-days=<days>` is unchanged. Items trashed before 6.0 have no trash time and go on the first run (see the
  guide). Guide: [The trash view](../../bc/6.0/trash.md#keeping-items-in-the-trash-for-a-while). Test:
  `TrashPurgeKeepDaysTest` (no database). The cronjob reads the setting past the INI cache, so a value just added
  counts on the next run instead of the cached "purge everything".

### Fixed

- `bin/php/trashpurge.php --trashed-days` with a huge number of days purged the whole trash: the date overflowed
  into the future. Some numbers overflow into a past that is not that many days back instead, which purged against
  the wrong date. `eZScriptTrashPurge::trashedBefore()` now accepts only a date within a day of that many days back
  and the purge refuses anything else with an error. Test: `TrashPurgeKeepDaysTest`.

## 9 October 2026: the HTTP cache with request-shield in front

### Added

- The role-aware HTTP cache works with [request-shield](https://github.com/cjw-network/request-shield) in front: a
  request it marks `allow-uncached` (`$_SERVER['REQUEST_SHIELD']`) is answered and never stored (`X-Exp-Cache:
  BYPASS (request-shield: not for a cache)`), and with `set cache-unknown-query hit-only` the early exit answers a
  request with a query parameter no key holds from the page request-shield names in `REQUEST_SHIELD_CACHE_LOOKUP`
  (`X-Exp-Cache: HIT (request-shield lookup)`). Without request-shield nothing changes. Under Velocity only the
  first applies. Guide: [With request-shield in front](../../bc/6.0/httpcache.md#with-request-shield-in-front).
  Test: `ezpHttpCacheContractTest` (HC-18 to HC-20).

### Updated

- With `ContentChangePurges=tags` a content change also purges `rl` of every node and `r` of every object, the tags
  Ibexa's HTTP cache purges, for pages an extension tagged that way. More than 10000 purged tags within `MaxAge` now
  purge every page instead, so the state every lookup reads stays small. Test: `ezpHttpCacheContractTest` (HC-21).

### Fixed

- The lookup of `REQUEST_SHIELD_CACHE_LOOKUP` is taken only when it names exactly the request's own path and
  parameters that `QueryStringParameters[]` lists: a request-shield whose `cache-query` left one of them out had
  `/news?page=2&x=7` answered with `/news`. Its answer is `Cache-Control: private`, so a proxy in front keeps no
  made-up address, and an expired page is served to it as `STALE` without the lookup taking the page's refresh,
  which it would never store.

## 9 October 2026: the RAD survey's source sweep skips dependency trees

### Updated
- Setup > RAD no longer walks into `tests`, `vendor`, `node_modules` or `.git` directories when it collects the php
  files of the kernel, lib and the extensions. It already ignored what it found there, but it still entered them,
  and every check of the kept sweep looked at each of their directories again: an extension that carries the
  `node_modules` of its JavaScript build doubled the directories checked (8,558 to 4,146 on one installation), and the first
  load after a cache clear ran long enough to time out. The php files collected are the same.

## 9 October 2026: the online editor keeps an embedded gallery's view mode

### Updated
- `settings/content.ini` lists `gallery_view_mode` among the custom attributes of `[embed]`. The embed view of a
  gallery reads it (slider or grid), but the online editor keeps only the custom attributes content.ini names, so
  every save of an article dropped it and the gallery fell back to the grid. Installations and the site settings
  the installers write add their own `[embed]` attributes to this one, so every siteaccess gets it.

## 10 October 2026: the content finder

### Added
- **Setup > Content finder** (`/contentfinder/search`, kernel module `contentfinder`) and a search box below the
  content structure in the Content tab: find the content object behind an object, node, attribute or version id, a
  remote id, a URL alias, a page URL, or the path or URL of an image, binary or media file below `var/` (exactly, or
  every file below a prefix). Each result shows the object's metadata, every version with a link to the version view,
  every location with its URL alias, and its trash entry (where it was, when and by whom it was trashed). From the
  result an object can be moved to the trash, deleted permanently, purged from the trash or restored, and a version
  removed, each through a confirmation page that lists every location, the nodes below them and the versions; what
  cannot be undone needs an explicit tick. The actions use the kernel's own operations (`content/delete`, the trash
  service's purge, `content/restore`, version removal as in the history).
- The policies `contentfinder/search` (use the finder) and `contentfinder/delete` (its actions). Each result shows
  only what the user's content policies allow (`content/read`, `content/versionread`, `content/restore` or
  `content/cleantrash`), and each action needs its content policies as well; something the user may not see is
  answered exactly like a miss. Only the Administrator role holds the new policies in a new installation.
- `settings/contentfinder.ini` (`Enabled`, `SiteAccessList[]`, `ResultLimit`, `FileRowLimit`): the finder answers on
  administration siteaccesses only.
- The audit events `content.finder.search` and `content.finder.action` (channel `content`, on by default): every
  search with its type, text and number of results, every action done or refused, with the kernel's removal events
  as its children. The catalogue has 143 names.
- German translations of the finder.
  [Feature](../../features/6.0/content-finder.md). Tests: `expContentFinderInputTest` (no database),
  `expContentFinderSandboxTest` (writes, so only in a throwaway copy named by `EXP_CONTENTFINDER_SANDBOX`);
  `--testsuite kernel-contentfinder`.

## 10 October 2026: a per-request file named in config.php

### Added
- `index.php` runs the file that `config.php` names in the constant `EXP_REQUEST_PREPEND_FILE` at the start of every
  request, after the settings and before the kernel. Under a persistent application server (Exponential Velocity)
  `config.php` runs once per worker, so code there cannot act on each request; this is the place for a request filter
  in front of the site. Only `config.php` can name the file. Without the constant nothing changes.
### Updated
- `config.php-RECOMMENDED` documents `EXP_REQUEST_PREPEND_FILE`, with request-shield under Velocity as the example,
  names the product Exponential in its comments and gives the real default of `CLUSTER_HEADER_X_POWERED_BY`.

## 11 October 2026: the content finder by the address a visitor sees, on every database engine

### Updated
- The content finder finds a page by the address a visitor sees on a siteaccess with a `PathPrefix`: `/site/showcase/test-product`,
  its full URL and the bare `showcase/test-product` now find the alias stored as `fit-healthy/showcase/test-product`.
  The siteaccess is matched as the kernel matches it (URI, host, host and URI), its own `PathPrefix` is put in front
  (honouring `PathPrefixExclude[]`), and a bare alias is tried with each distinct prefix after the exact match;
  **Found by** names the siteaccess and prefix. Tests: `expContentFinderURLAliasTest`.
- The content finder matches remote ids and file paths in their case on every database engine: MySQL and MariaDB
  compare without case, and so did a file prefix on SQLite. Its database tests run on MySQL/MariaDB, PostgreSQL,
  Oracle and MongoDB too (in a throwaway database named by `EXP_CONTENTFINDER_SANDBOX_DATABASE`); the test fixtures
  use single-table queries. Tests: `expContentFinderSandboxTest` CS-12.
- The content finder finds a URL alias by its last part (`test-product`) or a shorter tail (`showcase/test-product`)
  when nothing else resolves: every alias element with that text, checked upwards against the rest of the path,
  listed with its full path; old addresses (history) only when nothing current matches, and marked. Exact
  comparisons only. Tests: `expContentFinderSandboxTest` CS-13.
- MongoDB: the driver translates `LIKE '…' ESCAPE '!'` (the content finder's file prefixes found nothing) and
  `LOWER( column ) = '…'` / `UPPER`; the persistent object fetch on MongoDB compares a `LOWER( login )` condition in
  the server, so `eZUser::fetchByName()` and `fetchByEmail()` find the user again; `eZURLAliasML::fetchByPath()`
  (and `fetchNodeIDByPath()`) reads a path one element at a time instead of the join the driver refuses. Tests:
  `expMongoDBWhereTextTest`, `expContentFinderSandboxTest` on MongoDB.
- The content finder finds a URL alias by a part of it when nothing else matches: `product`, `test` or
  `test product` find `fit-healthy/showcase/test-product` (words in order, any of `-`, `_` and space between, without
  case; `%`, `_` and quotes are text). Current entries first, history only when nothing current matches; the
  closest matches first; at least 3 characters (with a hint below that). New `contentfinder.ini` settings
  `PartialAliasSearch`, `MinimumPartLength`, `PartRowLimit`. The MongoDB driver translates `LOWER( column ) LIKE`.
  Tests: `expContentFinderURLAliasTest` CU-06, `expContentFinderSandboxTest` CS-14, `expMongoDBWhereTextTest`.
- The content finder guide lists PostgreSQL 13, MongoDB and Oracle 26ai among the tested databases, with the cost
  of the part search on each. [Feature](../../features/6.0/content-finder.md#databases).

## 11 October 2026: a JSON format for packages

### Added
- A package can be kept as `package.json` and JSON documents instead of `package.xml` and XML documents. Every XML
  document of the package (the definition, the files of its items, the object files) is mirrored element for element
  in JsonML, rich text kept as its XML string, so the package handlers and datatypes read the same DOM from either
  format and a package converts from XML to JSON and back without loss. The classic format stays the default; a
  classic package, its archive and its installation are unchanged. `share/package/schema/package-v1.json` is the JSON
  Schema of the format (`expJsonSchemaValidator`), and `package.json` carries the SHA-256 of every file.
- `expPackageJson` (mapping, conversion of directories and archives, validation, checksums) and
  `expPackageConflictReport` (what an install meets here, by remote id). `eZPackage` exports in either format, detects
  the format of an import by its content and keeps package metadata (`<metadata><entry key>`) for tools.
- **Install as drafts**, a generic install option: content objects become drafts, nothing is published and no
  published object changes; an existing object gets a new draft or is left alone, an object of another class is a
  conflict; other items are listed instead of installed.
- `ezpm export --format=json`, `ezpm convert`, `ezpm validate`, `ezpm check` and `ezpm install --as-drafts`.
- Setup > Packages: **Download as JSON**, the package's format and metadata, the install page's report by remote id
  and the option to install as drafts. The upload check and the package catalog accept `package.json`.
- `settings/package.ini [PackageFormatSettings]` (`RawXMLDatatypes[]`, `MaxDocumentBytes`).
  Guide: [The JSON package format](../../bc/6.0/package-json-format.md). Test: `expPackageJsonTest`.

### Updated
- The JSON package format page shows rich text without the editor's namespace URIs.

## 11 October 2026: headers an extension decides per page in the HTTP cache

### Added
- The filter event `httpcache/store/headers`: a listener (`site.ini [Event] Listeners[]=httpcache/store/headers@<callback>`)
  gets the headers the role-aware HTTP cache keeps with a page when it stores it, and returns them with its own
  added. A page answered from the cache is assembled without the kernel, from the headers stored with it; those were
  only the content type and the security headers, so a header an extension adds for one page (the content license
  of xrowmetadata, for example) was missing on every cache hit.
  Guide: [Headers an extension adds to a page](../../bc/6.0/httpcache.md#headers-an-extension-adds-to-a-page).

## 11 October 2026: Exponential Velocity streams, deploys without a needless reload, and starts each worker clean

### Added
- `settings/velocity.ini [ServerSettings]` documents `StreamTimeout` (seconds a stream may run instead of the request
  timeout; engine default 300), `StreamShare` (the share of the workers event streams may hold; 0.25), `MaxStreams`
  (the same as a number of workers) and `RetryWithin` (seconds within which a GET whose worker died is run again,
  once; 10), and `exp:velocity` passes them to the engine. All are empty by default, so the engine's own defaults
  apply. Past the stream cap an event stream is answered 503 with `Retry-After`, so pages keep the rest of the pool.
  Guide: [Streaming responses](../../bc/6.0/velocity-engines.md#streaming-responses).
- `[DeploySettings] WarmUpURL[]`: the last step of `exp:velocity deploy` requests each URL once, one after the other,
  from a detached background process, so the first visitor does not rebuild the compiled templates and the INI,
  override, translation, design and packed-file caches. The deploy does not wait for it.

### Updated
- `[DeploySettings] PhpFpmReload=auto` (the default) skips the PHP-FPM reload when the pool's OPcache revalidates
  files (`validate_timestamps` on, `revalidate_freq` at most 60 s) and waits `revalidate_freq` plus one second
  instead, so the rendered-output caches are still cleared only after PHP-FPM runs the new code. A graceful reload
  waits for the longest request on every pool of that PHP version, which made the first admin request after a deploy
  take 50 to 100 s. `always` and `never` are the other values; `deploy --fpm-reload` forces a reload (php.ini or pool
  changed, new extension, files removed or renamed in place). Guide: [Deploying](../../guides/deploying.md).
- The Velocity warm-up clears the response it rendered (its state, the response object, the headers and the status)
  before the workers are forked. The first request of every worker began with the front page's headers already set,
  so a sign-in page, an access-denied answer or a JSON call could go out with the front page's content license,
  `X-Exp-Cache: MISS` and `Cache-Control`.
- The users and access services of expservices declare their lists in the class body instead of assigning them after
  the class: a persistent Velocity worker puts a class's statics back to their declared value before its next
  request, so from a worker's second request on those 207 services answered "not declared". Test:
  `expUsersDeclarationsTest`. Guide: [Backend ezjscore services](../../bc/6.0/backend_ezjscore_services.md).

## 11 October 2026: read services as tools of the assistant

### Added
- A service declaration of expservices may carry `'ai' => true` (or `'ai' => array( 'summary' => '...' )`): the
  assistant of [Exponential Intelligence](../../features/6.0/extensions/expai.md) may then call it. One declaration
  serves HTTP (`ezjscore/call`), MCP (the tools expmcp generates) and the assistant, each with the caller's own
  permissions. Services stay private to the assistant by default, and a service that writes is never offered; the
  catalogue reports the flag as `ai`. Opted in, as reads checked by the content's own read access: `expnode`
  `childrenNames`, `childrenCount`, `breadcrumb`, `tree`, `latest` and `find`; `expsection` `list` and `ofNode`;
  `expstate` `groups` and `ofObject`; `exptags` `roots`, `children`, `tree`, `search` and `ofnode`.
  Guide: [Backend ezjscore services](../../bc/6.0/backend_ezjscore_services.md).

## 11 October 2026: the online editor's engine page

### Updated
- `ezoe/engine` lists every entry of `ezoe.ini [EditorSettings] Engines[]` with its name, version, description and
  status (available, in use now, not available because its files are missing, or why it cannot be used), so an engine
  an extension registers (Tiptap, for example) shows up with what it is. An engine may answer the optional `version()`
  and `description()`; `expOEEditorEngineBase` answers both from `[Engine_<id>] Version` and `Description`, and the
  TinyMCE 3 and 8 engines read their version from the bundled script. English and German translations.
  Guide: [The online editor engines](../../bc/6.0/ezoe-tinymce8.md). Tests: `expOEEditorRegistryTest`,
  `expOEEngineViewTest`.

## 11 October 2026: fixes found by the CI workflows

### Updated
- The setup's package download and the Solr check read the HTTP status on PHP 8.0 to 8.3 too: they open the URL with
  `fopen()` and take the headers from the stream's `wrapper_data`. On those versions a failed package download said
  "HTTP 0" and the Solr check called a running Solr unreachable.
- The class-based views `content/versionview`, `error/view` and `user/forgotpassword` read their optional view
  parameters (`FromLanguage`, `ExtraParameters`, `HashKey`) from the view's scope, which static analysis can follow;
  no change in behavior.
- Tests that expected old contracts: the REST layer's OAuth token test expects the `Bearer` scheme it reads since the
  personal API keys (and that a personal key is never taken as an OAuth token), and the older MongoDB adapter test
  expects the statements the driver translates now.
- The eng-US, German and untranslated translations carry the Syndication tab, the debug quick settings of the
  administration and the content finder's Setup entry and page path, so the debug output lists no missing
  translation for them.

## 12 October 2026: the autoload generator leaves out the tests of every extension

### Fixed

- `bin/php/ezpgenerateautoloads.php -e` and `-o` took the `tests/` directory of every extension into
  `var/autoload/ezp_extension.php` and `ezp_override.php`. The exclusion compared the extension's path as the root
  gives it (`extension/foo`, relative) with the files, which are listed under their real path, so it never matched.
  A test bootstrap that defines stand-ins for `eZDB`, `eZINI` or `eZSys` then became a kernel override, and with
  `EZP_AUTOLOAD_ALLOW_KERNEL_OVERRIDE` the site loaded those stand-ins. `eZAutoloadGenerator` now anchors the
  exclusion at the real path of the extension, as it already did for `--exclude` and `.autoloadignore`. Run
  `php bin/php/ezpgenerateautoloads.php -e` (and `-o` where kernel overrides are allowed) once after the update.
  Test: `eZAutoloadGeneratorTest`.

### Updated

- The exclusion names the directory itself: `tests/` of an extension followed by the separator or the end of the
  path, so `tests_old/`, `testsuite/` or a file `tests.php` stay in the arrays. An extension whose path does not
  resolve is skipped (it lists no files anyway) instead of getting an expression built from the path as given;
  `--exclude` and `.autoloadignore` quote that fallback too. Test: `eZAutoloadGeneratorTest`.
- `tests/` and `Tests/` of a Composer package vendored inside an extension
  (`extension/<name>/vendor/<vendor>/<package>/tests`) are left out of `ezp_extension.php` and `ezp_override.php` as
  well: test cases and fixtures of that package, never runtime classes, and not in the package's own Composer
  autoloader either. Test: `eZAutoloadGeneratorTest`. On one installation the extension array went
  from 2481 to 2272 entries: 154 classes of extensions' `tests/` and 55 of a vendored package's tests left it,
  nothing else changed.
- Test classes load through a tests autoload instead of the runtime arrays. `ezpAutoloader::enableTests()` adds the
  test array `var/autoload/ezp_tests.php` (`ezpgenerateautoloads.php -s`), and `ezpAutoloader::enableTests( $dir )`
  the classes declared under a tests directory, read there and then (`eZAutoloadGenerator::testsClassMap()`), so a
  class added a moment ago is found without generating anything. The kernel's PHPUnit bootstrap
  (`tests/bootstrap.php`, also used by `tests/runtests.php`) turns it on; `ezexec.php` turns it on for a script that
  lies in a `tests/` directory (`php bin/php/ezexec.php extension/foo/tests/bar_test.php` finds the classes of
  `extension/foo/tests`); an extension's own PHPUnit bootstrap calls it with its tests directory.
  The site itself no longer loads `ezp_tests.php`; `define( 'EZP_AUTOLOAD_TESTS', true );` in `config.php` loads it
  everywhere as before. Tests: `eZAutoloadGeneratorTest`.

### Operator note

Run `php bin/php/ezpgenerateautoloads.php -e` once after the update (and `-o` where `config.php` allows kernel
overrides with `EZP_AUTOLOAD_ALLOW_KERNEL_OVERRIDE`), then reload PHP-FPM and restart Velocity
(`./console exp:velocity deploy` does all of it). Check afterwards that `grep -c "/tests/" var/autoload/ezp_extension.php`
prints 0. Test scripts and suites of an extension that relied on their classes being in `ezp_extension.php` load
them through the tests autoload above; run `ezpgenerateautoloads.php -s` where the kernel's own tests are run.

## 12 October 2026: a user account that exists only as a draft

### Fixed

- The account of a user object whose `ezuser` row is not stored yet, but whose edit left a draft (login, e-mail
  address, password hash) in the attribute, now shows that draft: `eZUserType::objectAttributeContent()` returns a
  new account with the draft applied instead of `null`, so the edit form keeps what was entered and the object has
  a title. Without a draft it stays `null` as before. Storing the first version of such an account and publishing
  its draft create the `ezuser_setting` row that a new account needs to sign in, enabled, unless it has one
  already (a disabled one stays disabled). Test: `eZUserDatatypeDraftTest` (no database).

### Updated

- The setting row of a new account follows one decision wherever it is made (the default account of a user object
  without one, the first version of an account, a published draft): an existing `ezuser_setting` row is never
  changed, so an account an administrator disabled, or one waiting for its activation, stays disabled. The default
  account used to store a new, enabled setting row over an existing one each time an object without an `ezuser`
  row was stored. A new row is enabled, as before, and disabled when the account has an account key
  (`ezuser_accountkey`, waiting for activation). Activation itself is unchanged: `user/register` disables the row
  and stores the key ([UserSettings] `VerifyUserType`), `user/activate` enables it.
- A draft becomes a new account only with a login, and only when no other account has that login (or its e-mail
  address, where `RequireUniqueEmail=true` and `AuthenticateMatch` includes `email`), the checks of the edit form.
  The draft may be older than the account that took them, or not come from the form at all: a copy of another
  user's object carried that user's draft (the published attribute keeps one after `user/password`), and storing
  the copy made a second account with the same login and password, able to sign in. Such a draft is not applied:
  the object keeps no account, the draft is cleared and the reason is logged as an error.
- A copy of a user object into a new object (`content/copy`) leaves the original's draft behind
  (`eZUserType::initializeObjectAttribute()`); a new version or translation of the same object keeps it.
- What counts as a draft is narrower: the four fields as strings, numbers or null (not `true`/`false`), a hash
  type that `eZUser` knows (`PASSWORD_HASH_*`), and a plaintext "hash" only on a site with `HashType=plaintext`,
  where on any other site it would set a password nobody chose. Anything else in `data_text` (invalid JSON, a
  list, a serialized PHP array, a login of blanks) makes no account and writes nothing; publishing it created a
  setting row for an object without account. The draft holds the password hash, never the password.
- Telling a new account from a stored one when the first version is stored keeps its lookup of the `ezuser` row
  by its primary key: the `eZUser` in hand does not say whether it was read or made, and the first version is
  stored only while the account is created. The login (and e-mail) check and the account key lookup run only for
  a new account. Tests: `eZUserDatatypeDraftSecurityTest` (no database), `eZUserDatatypeDraftTest`.

### Operator note

Nothing to run. User objects without an `ezuser` row, or accounts without an `ezuser_setting` row, are left as they
are: an account without a setting row cannot sign in (the sign-in fails closed) and `user/setting` cannot change it
either, so check for such accounts after importing users by SQL and give them a setting row on purpose. Setting
rows that belong to no object (left by an import or a removal outside `eZUser::removeUser()`) grant nothing; a new
user object whose id one of them has keeps that row as it is, where the default account used to enable it.

## 12 October 2026: faster Setup > Extensions, Info > About and audit dashboard

### Updated

- `eZExtension::extensionInfo()` no longer walks an extension's whole tree on every call to find its newest file
  ("Modified" on Setup > Extensions). The walk stats every file (52 000 files for 85 extensions here, close to a
  second) and Setup > Extensions, Info > About, the Online Editor dialogs and the update inventory asked it on every
  view. `eZExtension::newestFileTime()` keeps the result in `<cache>/exp/extension-file-times.json` for an hour and
  walks again before that when the active extensions cache is cleared (`ezcache --clear-tag=ini`, `--clear-all`,
  `exp:velocity deploy`, a change on Setup > Extensions); the extension's own `extension.xml`, `ezinfo.php` and
  `composer.json` are looked at every time. Setup > Extensions went from 1.0 s to 0.3 s, Info > About from 0.6 s
  to 0.1 s. Test: `eZExtensionNewestFileTimeTest`.
- The audit dashboard sums the records per channel of today and of the week from the per-day counts its chart
  reads, instead of two groupings that read the whole index table on SQLite (half a second each with 240 000
  records). `expAuditQuery` finds a family of event names (`access.*`, `system.expai.request.*`) on SQLite through
  a range of the name index together with the `LIKE`, which SQLite cannot plan with that index: the AI panel's
  counts went from 280 ms each to 2 ms. Other engines keep the `LIKE`. A dashboard built anew (its figures are kept
  a minute) takes about 1.5 s instead of 4 to 8 s. Test: `expAuditDashboardVolumeTest`.

## 12 October 2026: one count of extensions on the dashboard, Info > About and the update dashboard

### Fixed

- The administration showed three different numbers of extensions for one installation: the dashboard 61, Info >
  About 63 and the update dashboard "85 Extensions, 64 active", each counting something else under the same word.
  The dashboard counted `ActiveExtensions` only; About added the siteaccess's own `ActiveAccessExtensions` (here
  `expmcp` and `expai`, switched on for the admin and editor siteaccesses only); the update dashboard counted every
  directory in `extension/` and called active what any siteaccess switches on (adding `exp_adminui`, active in the
  `adminui` siteaccess only). All three now take their figures from `eZExtension::extensionCounts()` and say what
  they count: "63 active in this siteaccess, 85 on disk" on the dashboard and About, "85 Extensions on disk, 64
  active in any siteaccess" on the update dashboard and its installed packages page.

### Added

- `eZExtension::extensionLists()` and `eZExtension::extensionCounts()`: the extensions of the installation by kind,
  `on_disk` (every directory of every extension root, `ExtensionDirectory` and `AdditionalExtensionDirectories`,
  each name once), `global` (`ActiveExtensions`), `here` (active in the current siteaccess, in loading order),
  `anywhere` (active in at least one siteaccess of `AvailableSiteAccessList`), `inactive` and `missing` (named in
  the settings, not on disk). Every active kind counts extensions found on disk only. Template fetch:
  `fetch( 'setup', 'extension_counts', hash( 'all_siteaccesses', true() ) )`; without `all_siteaccesses` the kinds
  that read every siteaccess's settings (`anywhere`, `inactive`) are left out, which keeps the dashboard at 0.5 ms
  for the count. Test: `eZExtensionCountsTest`.

### Updated

- Info > About lists the extensions of `eZExtension::extensionLists()` `here`, in the loading order the kernel uses
  (`ExtensionOrdering`), where it listed `ActiveExtensions` and then `ActiveAccessExtensions`.
- The update dashboard's inventory reads every extension root, and an extension named in the settings but not on
  disk is listed under "Need attention" only, no longer counted as an extension or as active.

## 12 October 2026: Info > About and Copyright select the Setup tab

### Fixed

- `ezinfo/about` and `ezinfo/copyright` had no `default_navigation_part`, so the administration fell back to the
  first navigation part and highlighted "Content structure" with the content tree as the left menu. Both views now
  name `ezsetupnavigationpart`: the Setup tab is selected and the Setup menu is shown, as on the other pages about
  the installation. `ezinfo/is_alive` answers with plain text and is unchanged.

## 12 October 2026: Exponential Intelligence in the distribution

### Added
- `composer.json` requires `se7enxweb/expai` `~0.0.1.0`: **Exponential Intelligence**, the writing assistant of the
  administration interface ([feature page](../../features/6.0/extensions/expai.md), its own documentation in the
  [expai repository](https://github.com/se7enxweb/expai)). Composer puts it in `extension/expai`; it stays inactive
  until it is activated for the admin siteaccesses, its tables are created and a provider is configured. It writes
  drafts as the signed-in editor, with that editor's permissions, and never publishes on its own.
- The job queue `se7enxweb/expqueue` `~0.1.0` (required since 11 October), which runs the queued work of Exponential
  Intelligence and of any extension that registers job types ([feature page](../../features/6.0/extensions/expqueue.md)).
- The kernel parts the assistant builds on are generic and documented on their own: extension sources of the audit
  console (below), registries declared in `rad.ini` (below), read services opted in with `'ai' => true`
  (11 October), install as drafts and the JSON package format (11 October), the event `package/install/drafts`
  (below), and the marked version in the admin4 node header (below).

## 12 October 2026: Setup > Packages end to end

### Added
- **Validation of every upload** before it is imported: each document well formed, the JSON documents against the
  schema, the checksums of `package.json`. A package that fails is refused with the list of what failed; the result
  is shown on the package's install and view pages until the next action on it. **Validate** on a package's page
  runs the same validation (a POST, then a redirect back) and shows what was checked or every problem.
- **Install in the background**: the content job type `packageinstall` (`expContentJobPackageInstall`,
  `content.ini [ContentJobSettings] JobTypes[packageinstall]`) installs a package with the wizard's choices as the
  requesting user. The install page offers it, and suggests it above the content jobs' `SynchronousLimit`; the
  package's page shows its progress object by object and lists the drafts when it is done.
- The install page's report lists every object with what the install does with it and where its main location goes
  (at the top, below a node here, below a node of the package, or below a missing one), with the counts of each.
- The notify event `package/install/drafts` (`eZPackage::notifyDraftsInstalled()`) after every install as drafts:
  the admin's, `eZPackage::install()` (`ezpm`) and a background install, so an extension can mark or record the
  drafts. For the command line and the background worker the listeners of this event are attached from
  `site.ini [Event] Listeners[]`.
- The contents browser lists JSON documents by their real kind (Package definition, Content object, Content class)
  and shows them as JSON or as the XML they mirror, and an XML document as JSON; long lines wrap, with a No wrap
  switch. What a file is comes from its content: PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP images are shown inline, SVG only as text,
  any other binary as a card with its type, size, SHA-256 and whether it matches `package.json`.
  `package/viewfile` sends only such images inline, everything else as an attachment.
- **New package** asks in its information step which format the package is kept in (Classic XML or JSON); a JSON
  package is converted in place when the wizard finishes (`expPackageJson::convertInPlace()`).

### Updated
- The content handler reads the remote id of a per-object file of any length (only an md5's 32 characters were read,
  so objects with readable remote ids missed their post-install step) and reports its progress to an install that
  asks for it.
- The documents of a package (its license) open in the package's contents browser
  (`package/view/full/<name>/<repository>/(file)/<index>`) instead of a link into the storage, which the web server
  answered with 404.
  Guides: [Packages](../../guides/packages.md), [The JSON package format](../../bc/6.0/package-json-format.md#setup--packages).
  Test: `eZPackageFileBrowserJsonTest`.

## 12 October 2026: the audit console's extension sources, a streamed and background export, repeated records

### Added
- **Sources of extensions**: an extension that records its own audit events registers a source in
  `audit.ini [AuditSourceSettings] Sources[<identifier>]` with a class implementing `expAuditSource` (best extending
  `expAuditSourceBase`). The dashboard shows a panel per readable source (cached a minute per user); console, recent,
  charts, alerts and export show a line of its links; the charts draw its series per day; an event of its channel
  shows the source's fields and links; alerts name the source of a rule; Setup of the audit lists the registered
  sources and what keeps one from working; the `Channel` limitation of `audit/read` names an extension's channel
  after its source. Without a registered source every view is as before. Exponential Intelligence (channel `ai`) is
  the reference source.
- A source declares its own **CSV columns** (`expAuditSource::exportColumns()`), added after the generic ones to the
  CSV of its channel in `audit/export` and `exp:audit export --channel=<channel> --format=csv`.
- The audit export runs as the content job **`auditexport`** (`expContentJobAuditExport`, "Export in the background",
  any of the three formats), up to `[AuditConsoleSettings] MaxBackgroundExportRecords` (1000000). The export page
  lists the user's background exports with their state and progress and offers the finished file at
  `audit/export/(download)/<id>`, only to the user who asked for it, recorded as `system.audit.read` with the job.
- `fetch( 'audit', 'hidden_menu_links' )`: the audit left menu leaves out a link into a channel the user may not read
  (an audit view filtered on the channel, or a link that `menu.ini` names with `AuditChannel_<key>=<channel>`).

### Updated
- The download of `audit/export` is **streamed** (`expAuditExportStream`): the file starts at once and is sent while
  it is read, also from a Velocity pool worker (`X-Accel-Buffering: no`). A large filter is read in order by id in
  segments instead of OFFSET pages: the content channel's CSV (26,889 records) took 38.3 s on Apache and was a 504 on
  Velocity; it now starts within about a second and ends in 3.3 to 4.1 s, with the same bytes.
- A process forked with `pcntl_fork()` no longer writes its parent's buffered audit records a second time: the child
  drops its copy on its first event, flush or reset and takes a request id of its own, and keeps the job, the run and
  the parent stack. Before, the same records reached the channel once per child, each copy with a new `seq` and hash.
- `exp:audit verify` reports lines that repeat a record id as a `repeated_ids` notice per file (not a break) and
  prints the distinct count next to the record count; `exp:audit export` leaves a repeated id out, as the index does.
- A test run writes its audit records into a directory of its own under `var/tmp`
  (`expAuditConfig::setTestLogDir()`, set by `tests/bootstrap.php`), never into the site's live audit trail.
- The alerts of the audit dashboard break rule names only at their underscores and say "1 address" and "1 problem"
  for one.
  Guide: [The audit guide](../../bc/6.0/audit.md) (extension sources, [streaming the export](../../bc/6.0/audit.md#streaming-the-export),
  [repeated records](../../bc/6.0/audit.md#repeated-records)). Tests: `expAuditSourceTest`,
  `expAuditExportStreamTest`, `expAuditForkTest`.

## 12 October 2026: registries declared in rad.ini

### Added
- **setup/rad counts the registries that the kernel and extensions declare in `rad.ini`**: `[RegistrySettings]
  Registries[]` and a `[Registry_<key>]` block naming the INI blocks (`Ini`, `Section`, `Entries[<variable>]=<check>`)
  or the class list (`List`, `Implements`). The survey counts every entry, checks it (a class or interface, callable,
  listener, template, `section:`, `in:`, `one-of:`, name) and shows a failing entry or declaration as broken;
  `InTotal=disabled` lists a registry without adding it to the total. Only active extensions are read.
  `settings/rad.ini` declares the kernel's event listeners, cronjob part scripts and audit console sources.
- The survey counts every policy limitation of every module, with its class, and its event sweep finds events
  announced on a variable or property holding `ezpEvent` (`package/install/drafts` and others); the catalogue has
  the mechanisms registry and filter.

### Updated
- ezjscore (server functions, key `ezjscserver`) and ezoe (editor engines, key `ezoeengines`) declare their
  registries in their own `settings/rad.ini.append.php`; the survey's code no longer names them.
- A later file's `Registries[]` (or `Sections[]` in a registry's block) without a value empties the list, as it does
  in the settings.
- `rad.ini` declarations are no longer counted as settings.
  Guides: [The RAD extension surface](../../bc/6.0/rad-extension-surface.md),
  [RAD extension points](../../bc/6.0/rad-extension-points.md). Tests: `RadSurveyDeclaredRegistriesTest`,
  `RadSurveyRegistriesTest`.

## 12 October 2026: Setup > System information under Velocity

### Updated
- Setup > System information shows the sizes of one shared measurement: `var/`, each cache directory and a database
  on a server are measured in one walk (within ten seconds) and kept for ten minutes in
  `var/<site>/cache/exp/system-report-sizes-<key>.json`, shared by PHP-FPM and Velocity. **Measure again** measures
  anew (posted with the form token), and the note under the buttons says when the sizes were measured. The page took
  about 2 s under Apache and 4 to 7 s under Velocity on every view. Each part of the report is a debug accumulator.
- The Velocity box reads the process table once and sends its own server no health check request
  (`expVelocity::status( false )`, `expVelocity::reuseProcessTable()`); it took 0.4 to 0.7 s of every view.
- The page lists a closure autoloader by its file and line: under Velocity `spl_autoload_functions()` includes a
  closure, which the template could not print, so the page answered 500 on the Velocity port.
  Guide: [System information](../../guides/system-information.md). Test: `expSystemReportTest`.

## 12 October 2026: smaller fixes in the administration

### Updated
- The PDF export's table operator counts its parameters (`count( $operatorParameters ) > 2`): it counted a boolean,
  which PHP 8 refuses with a `TypeError`, so every PDF export with a table answered 500.
  Guide: [PDF export](../../features/6.0/pdf-export.md#what-was-repaired).
- `user/preferences/set` opened directly, from a bookmark or from another site (`Sec-Fetch-Site` `none` or
  `cross-site`) shows a confirmation (`user/preferences_confirm.tpl` in design standard, admin and admin4) whose form
  posts with the form token, then stores the preference and returns to the page the address named. Before, it stored
  nothing and redirected to the front page. A click on the site's own link stores it at once, as before.
- The show/hide tabs button of the content view shows the icon of the state the tabs are in (admin, admin3, admin4);
  the two images were swapped.
- The version in the header of a node (admin4) is marked (`span.exp-object-version` with `data-object-id`, and
  `span.exp-object-version-number`), so a page script can show a newly published version without a reload.
- The extension file time cache sets its file mode through `eZFile::fileMode()` alone, so the site's mode limits
  apply.
- The editor siteaccess the setup makes keeps the node view tabs of its access extensions: its
  `AdditionalTabs[]` list (roles, policies, eztags, authors) is followed by the tabs each extension of the admin's
  `ActiveAccessExtensions[]` declares in its own `settings/admininterface.ini.append.php`
  (`eZStepCreateSites::editorAdditionalTabs()`). The editor's list starts empty and an access extension's settings
  are read before the siteaccess's, so expai was switched on there without its Intelligence tabs.
  Test: `EditorSiteaccessTabsTest`.
