- An installation without a package language map (`exp:install`, the kickstarter) falls back to the language of
  site package content that is in none of the chosen languages: the language is added after the clean data's to
  `SiteLanguageList` (no siteaccess is made for it) and exists before the import, so the import and the site package's
  steps find that content (`eZStepCreateSites::contentFallbackLanguages()`, reading every object of the content
  packages with `eZContentObjectPackageHandler::objectLanguageLists()`). The CJW Multisite starter content is in
  ger-DE with English translations of a fifth of it; installed with eng-US alone, the install logged 125 errors
  ("pathWithNames() failed to fetch path" for every image, the starter's hidden folder and privacy page "not
  installed") and the site could not show that content. Test: `SetupContentFallbackLanguagesTest`.
- The response cache clear that switching maintenance on starts (`exp:velocity cache clear`, a process of its own)
  carries the setup run's id when a setup run starts it (`EXP_SETUP_RUN`), and its audit entry looks no user up then
  (`expAudit::canLookUp()`): it ran with the settings of before the installation, and every installation logged four
  failed connections to the database those name. Test: `SetupRunCommandAuditTest`.
- A file attribute of a type without a text extraction handler (a video, an image, an archive) is indexed by its
  name alone with a notice, not a warning ("Mimetype video/mp4 not supported for indexing" on every publish of the
  multisite demo content's videos); a handler that is configured but missing still warns.

## 12 October 2026: the main branch resolves as 6.0.x-dev

### Updated
- `composer.json` carries the branch alias `"dev-main": "6.0.x-dev"`. Installed from `main`, the root package was
  `dev-main`, which satisfies no version constraint, so a package that requires `se7enxweb/exponential` `^6.0.14`

  from the environment and skip when they are not set: `EXP_TEST_MONGO_URI` and `EXP_TEST_MONGO_DATABASE` (default

## 12 October 2026: uncaught errors under Velocity, the rich text editor's upload form

### Updated
- An uncaught exception or error under Exponential Velocity gets the site's error page (error.ini
  `StaticErrorPage[500]`) with its reference, and the line in `error.log`, as under PHP-FPM.
  `eZExecution::registerShutdownHandler()` installs `defaultExceptionHandler` again whenever no exception handler is
  in place (`eZExecution::currentExceptionHandler()`): a persistent worker takes away the handler a request installed
  once the request has ended, while the class's statics keep the flag that it was installed, so from a worker's
  second request on such an error got the server's own error page and was written only to Velocity's console log.
  A handler other code installed is kept. Test: `eZExecutionExceptionHandlerTest`.
- The upload view of the rich text editor reads its text fields (`uploadUrl`, `location`, `objectName`,
  `ContentObjectAttribute_*`) through `Upload::postString()`: a field sent as a list (`name[]`) counts as empty,
  where it was a `TypeError` and HTTP 500. Test: `expOEUploadPostStringTest`.
- Upgrade note: a changed kernel class reaches Velocity only with a restart (`exp:velocity deploy`); until then its
  workers keep the classes they had loaded. A real PNG uploaded from the editor's dialog answered 500 on the Velocity
  port while the search index's new integer range check (`eZDBInterface::clampInteger()`) was on disk and the workers
  still had the database classes from before it; Apache, which loads every class again on each request, was not
  affected. The upload works on both servers after the deploy.

## 12 October 2026: clean-up handlers and shutdown functions for each request under Velocity

### Updated
- Under Exponential Velocity's persistent workers every request gets its own clean-up and fatal error handlers and
  its own shutdown functions (`eZExecution`'s clean-up handler and `eZSession::stop()`), as under PHP-FPM. A worker
  puts the statics back to what they were after its warm-up before each request, and the flag that the shutdown
  functions were registered was among them: from a worker's second request on none was registered (no clean-up and
  no error page after a fatal error), and every request ran the warm-up's handlers beside its own. The handlers, the
  clean exit flag and the shutdown functions now belong to one request (`eZExecution::requestKey()`, the request's
  `REQUEST_TIME_FLOAT`); a handler added twice in one request runs once; a shutdown function left by an earlier
  request (the warm-up's) does nothing (`eZExecution::runForRequest()`). The cluster file handler's cleanup handler,
  the SQL profile, the query cache's counters, the log context's repeat counts and the audit's handlers are
  registered per request the same way. Nothing changes under Apache/PHP-FPM or on the command line.
  Test: `eZExecutionPerRequestRegistrationTest`. Guide: [Velocity](../../features/6.0/velocity-persistent-worker-server.md).
- Upgrade note, for developers: a registration that must happen once per request is guarded with
  `eZExecution::requestKey()`, not with a `true` flag, which a worker keeps from its warm-up.
- Exponential Velocity 0.0.4.51 ends the warm-up's request itself before it forks its workers, for every application
  on the engine, not only this kernel: the shutdown functions the warm-up's render registered are dropped without
  running, and its headers, error and exception handlers, session, ini and environment changes and open output
  buffers are put back. A worker's first request (with ForkPerRequest, every request) used to run the warm-up's
  shutdown functions beside its own; `eZExecution::runForRequest()` stays as the kernel's own guard. A request's
  `$_SESSION` now also ends with the request, as under PHP-FPM.

- The version preview (`content/versionview`) of a version with more than one location marks the location it shows:
  the templates of every design read `$placement`, which the view did not hand over, so the error log
  had "Unknown template variable 'placement'" for each preview and no location was checked.
  `kernel/content/versionviewframe.php` sets it from the view's placement. Test: `eZContentVersionviewPlacementTest`.

## 13 October 2026: links to content of another site of a multisite installation

### Fixed
- A page of one site that lists content of another site of the same installation links to that content on the site
  that serves it. On the Bold Agency tag pages (`/bold/tags/view/Topics/HIIT` and seven more) every Fit & Healthy
  article linked to `/bold/fit-healthy/...`, which answered 404 (kernel (20), module not found); it now links to
  `/video/...` (or `/site/video/...` where Fit & Healthy is on `/site`). Links within a site, module links and every
  link of an administration siteaccess are unchanged.

### Added
- The extension `ezurlmultisite` (se7enxweb/ezurlmultisite 1.1.1) is required and activated by the multisite
  installers (`sevenx_multisite`, `sevenx_multisite_clean`, `cjw_multisite`). Their new post-install step
  `postInstallWriteMultisiteURLSettings` writes `settings/override/ezurlmultisite.ini.append.php`
  (`[MultisiteSettings] SiteAccessList[]`, `RootNodeList[]`) from the PathPrefix, `RootNode` and `IndexPage` the
  install wrote, so the node ids are those of the new installation.
- The media theme's own link operators (`exp_path`, `exp_url`, `content_link` ...) follow it
  (sevenx-themes-media 1.5.55).
- [Links to content of another site of a multisite installation](../../features/6.0/multisite-cross-site-links.md).

### Operator note
- An existing multisite installation adds `ActiveExtensions[]=ezurlmultisite` and its own
  `settings/override/ezurlmultisite.ini.append.php` (the page above), regenerates the extension autoloads and clears
  the INI, template and content caches. Without the settings file the public siteaccesses with a PathPrefix take
  part, with the root node of their `RootNode` or `IndexPage`.

## 14 October 2026: the query cache on Oracle

### Fixed
- On Oracle the query cache never hit across requests: ezoracle sets `NLS_NUMERIC_CHARACTERS`,
  `NLS_LENGTH_SEMANTICS` and `CURSOR_SHARING` with one `ALTER SESSION` on every connect, which
  `eZDBQueryCache::noteWrite()` counted as a write of unknown tables, so every request made every cached result
  stale. An `ALTER SESSION SET` of only session parameters that change a result by their value alone (`NLS_*`,
  `CURSOR_SHARING`, `TIME_ZONE`) is no write now (`eZDBQueryCache::isNeutralAlterSession()`); any other
  `ALTER SESSION` (`CURRENT_SCHEMA`, `EDITION`, `CONTAINER`, `EVENTS`, ...) still makes everything stale. Measured on
  Oracle 11g XE with ezoracle 2.3.6: from 2 hits of 12 lookups per request to 10 of 12. Test: `eZDBQueryCacheTest`
  QC-16.
- The parameters such a statement sets are part of the key of every statement of that connection
  (`eZDBQueryCache::sessionSettings()`, `sessionKey()`, kept per driver object): a result is answered only to a
  session with the same settings. With `OracleCaseInsensitive=enabled` the driver adds `NLS_COMP` and `NLS_SORT`, and
  the cache now hits across requests there too; a session with another `NLS_SORT` is never answered a result sorted
  by the first. Tests: QC-19, QC-20. The key of every statement changes once with this release, so results cached
  before it are not answered again (they expire after `MaxAge`).

## 14 October 2026: the query cache in the debug output

### Added
- With the debug output on, the query cache shows what it did for the request: the accumulators of the group
  *Query cache* (hits, misses, stores, statements not cached), a timing point where its state is read, and a
  *Query cache* report at the bottom with the request's counters, the hit rate, the database time the hits saved
  (each entry now records how long its statement took when stored), why statements were not cached and the writes
  that made everything stale (string literals masked). Switch: `querycache.ini [DebugSettings] Report` (default
  `enabled`); with the debug output off nothing is measured. Described in
  [the query cache page](../../bc/6.0/sql-query-cache.md#in-the-debug-output). Tests: `eZDBQueryCacheTest` QC-17, QC-18.
- The literal masking (`eZDBQueryCache::maskLiterals()`) reads a literal both as SQL-standard quoting (`''`) and as
  MySQL's backslash escapes (`\'`), and masks an unterminated one to the end, so no part of a literal reaches the
  report under either reading. Test: QC-21.
- After `eZDebug::reset()` in the same process (a worker between two jobs) the report starts again with its own
  counters, and the timing point comes again, at once when the process already holds the state. The report's
  counters are its own, so Setup > System information flushing the request's counters leaves them as they are; with
  the debug output switched off before a reset nothing is registered. Test: QC-22.
- With the debug output off the cache tests per statement only whether it is on, without a call (measured within the
  noise of a lookup hit).

## 14 October 2026: Oracle 11g and 12.1 (ezoracle 2.3.7)

### Fixed
- URL aliases resolve again on Oracle before 12.1 (11g): since ezoracle 2.3.1 every lookup by `text_md5`
  (`eZURLAliasML`, `eZURLAliasQuery`) failed with ORA-00904, as `STANDARD_HASH()` only exists from 12.1 on. Before
  12.1 the driver hashes the string literals the kernel passes in PHP, which is the value `text_md5` holds; from
  12.1 on nothing changes (se7enxweb/ezoracle pull request #2).
- The DFS cluster backend for Oracle lists its files with a limit before 12.1 too (ROWNUM instead of
  `OFFSET`/`FETCH`), and the driver reads the server version again after a reconnect.

### Updated
- `composer.json` requires `se7enxweb/ezoracle` `~2.3.7`.

## 14 October 2026: the query cache in the Exp Debug bar

### Added
- The Exp Debug bar shows the SQL query cache at a glance: a chip in the pinned bar with the request's hit rate and
  the database time it saved (`QC 83 % · -186 ms`), amber below 50 % or when a write made everything stale, red below
  10 % or in `shared` mode without APCu (`debugbar.ini [DebugBarSettings] Thresholds[querycache_hit_rate]`). The
  cache's report and accumulators stand in the SQL tab. The Cache tab shows what the server holds: APCu entries and
  memory, the counters since the last reset, the state, the cached results grouped by the tables they read (entries,
  rows, size, hits) and the largest entries, with buttons to clear the cache and reset the counters
  (`expdebugbar::cache by=querycache_counters`). New: `eZDBQueryCache::debugSnapshot()`, `inventory()`,
  `expDebugBarSummary::queryCacheLevel()`. Tests: `eZDBQueryCacheTest` QC-23, QC-24, `expDebugBarQueryCacheTest`.
- Refined on merging: the level of the chip and the figures of the summary have one source,
  `expDebugBarReport::queryCacheLevel()` and `queryCacheFigures()`, which `expDebugBarSummary` uses too (the bar's
  own fallback had a copy of the rules). `debugSnapshot()` gives the figures of the report's own counters and reads
  no state, so the chip and the report agree after `eZDBQueryCache::flushStats()` and after `eZDebug::reset()`.
  `inventory()` leaves out the state file's path itself. In the Cache tab the counters button reads "Reset", the
  section is read again after a clear or a reset, and the query cache is no longer listed a second time under
  "By ID". Tests: `eZDBQueryCacheTest` QC-23, QC-24 extended, `expDebugBarQueryCacheTest` DQ-05.
- The query cache tables of the Cache tab have a header row and their figures right aligned, from classes of
  `expdebugbar.css` with its light and dark tokens (no inline style); checked in a browser on Apache and Velocity at
  1440 px and at 960 px with scale 2, light and dark.

## 14 October 2026: the query cache in files

### Added
- `shared` mode keeps its results in files where there is no APCu: `querycache.ini [QueryCacheSettings] Storage`,
  `auto` (default: APCu where the PHP process has it, else files), `apcu` (only APCu, without it `shared` works as
  `request` as before) or `file` (always files, for web servers that share `var/` on NFS or a SAN). Upgrade note:
  a web server with `Mode=shared` and without APCu now writes files instead of keeping only the request's memo;
  schedule the cronjob part `cache_cleanup` or set `Storage=apcu` for the old behaviour. Cronjobs and scripts
  without APCu keep the memo only under `auto`. One file per
  result in `var/<site>/cache/querycache/entries/<2>/<key>`, read with `allowed_classes` off, written to a temporary
  file and renamed; age and state are checked as for APCu. New cronjob `querycache_cleanup.php` in the part
  `cache_cleanup` removes the files older than `MaxAge` or from before the last clear; a clear removes them at once.
  Setup > System information, the debug report, the Exp Debug bar's chip and Cache tab show the storage; the chip is
  red only when `shared` has no storage. New: `eZDBQueryCache::storage()`, `entriesDir()`, `cleanupFiles()`.
  Described in [the query cache page](../../bc/6.0/sql-query-cache.md#settings-settingsquerycacheini).
  Tests: `eZDBQueryCacheTest` QC-25 to QC-28, `expDebugBarQueryCacheTest`.
- Refined on merging: `Storage=auto` uses the files in the workers of Exponential Velocity too (their SAPI is `cli`,
  so they had kept only the memo); a result file is only ever named by a key (hex digits only); without the cronjob
  the requests sweep the files themselves, at most every 10 minutes (or `MaxAge`) for the installation, one process
  at a time and for at most half a second (`eZDBQueryCache::sweepFiles()`), so they cannot grow without bounds; the
  Exp Debug bar never receives the path of the result files. Tests: QC-29 to QC-31.

## 14 October 2026: an APCu reserve for the query cache

### Added
- The query cache leaves room in APCu for the PHP-FPM pool's other users: `querycache.ini [QueryCacheSettings]
  ApcuReserve` (percent, default 25, `0` off). With that share or less of APCu free, `shared` mode stops adding
  results to APCu and keeps them for the request only. APCu full with valid entries empties itself completely, and
  the HTTP cache, API keys or a request filter's counters in the same pool went with it. The free share is read from
  `apcu_sma_info()` before every store. Since APCu frees expired entries only when they are deleted by key or an
  allocation fails, below the reserve a miss deletes its key's old entry and `eZDBQueryCache::purgeExpired()` deletes
  the cache's expired entries once a minute for the pool (up to 30,000 APCu entries and below 80 % of `memory_limit`; in a
  web request after the page). Setup > System information and
  the Exp Debug bar's Cache tab count the results held back (counter `reserve`; `eZDBQueryCache::$stats` has the new
  key `reserve`), the debug report lists them. New: `eZDBQueryCache::apcuRoom()`, `purgeExpired()`.
  Tests: `eZDBQueryCacheTest` QC-32 to QC-34.
- Refined on merging: in a worker of Exponential Velocity the purge runs at the end of the request, registered once
  per request, instead of while the visitor waits (Velocity's SAPI is `cli`); a script still runs it at once. The
  APCu reserve warning of Setup > System information uses the shared admin components (`a4-notice is-warn`): its
  old class was no longer styled. `apcu_sma_info()` measured at 0.2 µs a store. Tests: QC-35,
  `AdminRetiredClassesTest`. Setup > System information and the Cache tab of the Exp Debug bar show the setting
  `Storage` next to the storage it gives, and `ApcuReserve` (`status()` has `storage_setting`).

## 14 October 2026: a resizable Exp Debug panel

### Added
- The open panel of the Exp Debug bar can be resized: a handle on its top edge is dragged with the mouse, a pen or a
  finger, or focused (`role="separator"` with `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuemax`) and moved with the
  arrow keys (16 px, with Shift 64 px; Home and End for the limits); a double-click gives the default height (60 % of
  the window) back. The height stays between 120 px and the window's height minus the bar, also when the window
  changes size. It is saved once at the end of a drag or 0.6 s after the last key, in the user preference
  `expdebugbar_panel_height` (new server function `expdebugbar::panelheight`, POST with the form token), and the
  report prints it on the panel as `data-exp-debug-panel-height`, so the panel opens at that height in any browser
  of the user. An anonymous visitor (the bar shown by IP) keeps the height in the browser's `localStorage`
  (`exp-debug-panel-height`, read back and clamped before the panel opens): no preference is read or saved for
  one and no request is sent, so the bar never starts a session; a logged-in user's preference is authoritative.
  New: `expDebugBarReport::panelHeight()`, `savedPanelHeight()`, three English and
  German texts. Guide: [The panel's height](../../bc/6.0/debug-bar.md#the-panels-height). Tests:
  `expDebugBarReportPanelHeightTest`, `expDebugBarReportCopyMessagesTest`.

### Updated
- The page cache (`exphttpcache`) no longer keeps a page that carries the debug report of its request
  (`X-Exp-Cache: BYPASS (debug report)`). A signed-in user's front page was answered from it with the report of an
  earlier request, so the resized debug panel opened at the height from before the last resize after a reload, the
  timings and queries were those of the stored request, and a report shown by IP could reach every visitor sharing
  the anonymous context. Test: `ezpHttpCacheContractTest` HC-23.
- The debug bar's Velocity tab says where the request entered and which process rendered the report, in place of
  "Not running on Velocity.": "Received by Velocity on port 8080 and rendered by a Velocity worker." or "Served by
  Apache / PHP-FPM (port 443), not by Velocity.", also for the Velocity parent, php-cgi started by Velocity, mod_php
  and FrankenPHP, with the rows Entered (port and forwarded headers), Received by, Rendered by, Rendered at and
  Cached. A report that still comes out of a cache is marked as such, with when and by which process it was rendered.
  On :8080 the tab had shown PHP-FPM: the signed-in front page came from the role-aware page cache, stored by PHP-FPM
  for a request on 443 and answered by Velocity from the same store. The OPcache box says when
  `opcache_get_status()` is switched off. English and German texts. Guide:
  [Velocity: where the request entered](../../bc/6.0/debug-bar.md#velocity-where-the-request-entered-and-what-rendered-it).
  Test: `expDebugBarReportEngineTest`.

### Fixed
- A page with the debug report is sent `Cache-Control: private, no-store`: Velocity's response cache had kept an
  anonymous page with the report of a request from an address in `DebugIPList` and answered every visitor of the
  Velocity port with it.

## 14 October 2026: the query cache's saved time against the page's time

### Added
- The query cache chip of the Exp Debug bar also gives the database time saved in percent of the page's time
  (`QC 83 % · -186 ms (186 %)`); its title says how long the page would have taken without the cache ("without the
  cache about 286 ms instead of 100 ms"). The debug report's row *Database time saved (estimated)* gives the same
  share of the request so far. A share above 100 % means the hits saved more than the page now takes. New:
  `eZDBQueryCache::savedShare()`, `debugSnapshot()` has `request_ms` and `saved_share`. Translated in ger-DE and eng-US.
  Tests: `expDebugBarQueryCacheTest` DQ-02, DQ-06, `eZDBQueryCacheTest` QC-17, QC-23.
- Refined on merging: the report row and the chip measure the same span (`eZDBQueryCache::requestMs()`): from the
  script start the web kernel sets for every request, also in a persistent worker of Exponential Velocity (else
  `REQUEST_TIME_FLOAT`), to where the bar's output starts (`setRequestEnd()`); a start that would give a negative
  time or more than a day gives no share. From 100 % on both also say how many times as long the page would have
  taken (`savedFactor()`: "286 ms, 3 times as long"), so "186 %" no longer reads like a part of the page. Translated
  in ger-DE and eng-US. Tests: QC-36, QC-37, DQ-07.

## 14 October 2026: Bootstrap Icons in admin4

### Added
- The icon theme `bootstrap-icons` (`share/icons/bootstrap-icons/`): Bootstrap Icons 1.13.1 as SVG, unchanged, with
  their MIT licence, for `class_icon`, `mimetype_icon`, `classgroup_icon`, `icon` and the content tree's
  `icon_info`. It maps every class and MIME type of `crystal-admin`, the classes of the website interface and demo
  packages and the OOXML, EPUB, RTF, CSV, XML, JSON and SVG types; one file serves every size. An admin siteaccess
  switches to it in its `icon.ini` (`Theme=bootstrap-icons`, `AdditionalThemeList[]=crystal-admin`); nothing
  changes until then. A class the theme does not map keeps its `crystal-admin` PNG in `class_icon`, but the content
  tree (`icon_info`, first theme only) shows the theme's `Default` for it: add it to `ClassMap`. Guide: [the admin4 design: icons](../../features/6.0/admin4-design.md#icons).
- The shared admin components `.a4-icon` with `.a4-bi-<name>` (a Bootstrap icon as a mask in the text colour,
  1em or 12, 16, 22 px) and `.a4-icon-btn` (a link or button that is only an icon), in both copies of
  `components.css`. Guide: [Styling the administration](../../bc/6.0/admin4-styling.md#components).

### Updated
- admin4 draws its icons with Bootstrap Icons in the text colour of the light and the dark mode: the messages, the
  sorted columns, the tabs switch, the sub-items table (row menu, image preview, the buttons above it and their
  menus), the search box, the context menus and the content tree (a caret right and down on the column's
  background, so the connector line no longer crosses the symbol) in its stylesheets, in place of the GIF and PNG
  sprites; edit, invert selection, up one level, trash, move, the current user and the inline SVGs of buttons,
  badges and the header in its templates. The classic designs keep their images.
- The image buttons of class editing (top, up, down, bottom) and of the Locations window (move) in admin4 are
  `<button>`s that post the same variables (`MoveUp` = the attribute id, as `eZHTTPTool` made of `MoveUp_<id>_x`).
  The invert selection images are buttons a keyboard reaches.
- In dark mode a filter turns the `<img>` icons of the `bootstrap-icons` theme light.
- Test: `AdminIconsTest` (every file the theme, a class or a stylesheet names exists; no file is left over; every
  file is an unchanged Bootstrap icon; admin4's templates draw no GIF icon, inline SVG or image button).

## 14 October 2026: language codes instead of flags in admin4

### Added
- `eZLocale` attribute `content_language_code` (`contentLanguageCode()`): the locale's language tag from its locale
  file (`[HTTP] ContentLanguage`, e.g. `de-DE`), which `site.ini [RegionalSettings] HTTPLocale` does not override.
- The include `design:parts/language_badge.tpl` and the shared admin components `.a4-lang` and `.a4-langs` in both
  copies of `components.css`. Guide: [the admin4 design: languages](../../features/6.0/admin4-design.md#languages).

### Updated
- admin4 shows a content language as a short code badge (`DE`, `EN`; `EN-GB` and `EN-US` when both exist) and its
  name instead of a flag: the node view's meta line, the sub-items table's Translations column, the Translations
  tab, the edit page and its language choice, version view, history, drafts, pending items, edit conflicts, URL
  aliases, states, classes and the top node. The badges have the language's name as title and, where it is not
  written next to them, as accessible name; the meta line marks the language shown with `aria-current` instead of
  dimming the others. The classic admin design shows the code in the sub-items table's Translations column too
  (its own copy of the include and of `ezajaxsubitems_expdatatable.js`, which stays identical to admin4's); its
  other templates and the other designs keep their flags.
- Test: `AdminLanguageBadgeTest` (no flag in an admin4 template or script, every badge through the include, the
  sub-items column escapes and names its badges, the component in both stylesheets, the include's optional
  parameters carry the prefix `badge_` and every include passes only known parameters, no flag in either design's
  sub-items table).

### Fixed
- `design:parts/language_badge.tpl` no longer reads a variable of the template that includes it: its optional
  parameters are `badge_name`, `badge_named`, `badge_plain` and `badge_class`. Called `class` and `name`, they read
  the content class of `class/view.tpl` and `class/edit.tpl`, and `class/view` ended in a fatal error (HTTP 500).

## 14 October 2026: admin4 context menus from the tokens and the keyboard

### Updated
- admin4's context menus (content tree, sub-items, class icon, bookmarks and their submenus) are drawn from the
  admin tokens: card, edge, radius and shadow of the light and dark mode, 14 px text in compact rows of about 27 px (at least
  24 px, the WCAG 2.2 target size), a
  width that fits the longest item up to 22rem (longer items end in an ellipsis), the title as a quiet row instead of a fixed grey bar (4.39:1), the accent tint
  instead of a blue for the hovered and focused row, a forced-colors fallback. Before, the shadow was a hard light
  grey that glowed around the menu in dark mode. Guide: [the admin4 design: context menus](../../features/6.0/admin4-design.md#context-menus).
- `ezpopupmenu.js` (admin4 and admin) gives the menus the roles of the WAI-ARIA menu pattern when it shows them and
  handles the keys: Enter, Space, Shift+F10 or the context menu key open a menu under its button with the focus on
  the first item; arrows, Home and End move; Arrow Right and Left open and close submenus; Escape closes and
  returns the focus; Tab closes. A menu opened with the mouse is reached with Arrow Down from its button. Its
  public functions are unchanged.
- The content tree's menu button is a named `role="button"` with `aria-haspopup="menu"`; the sub-items row button
  lets `ezpopupmenu.js` place a menu opened from the keyboard.
- A submenu opens beside its parent menu's right edge (the position used the submenu's own width).
- Test: `AdminContextMenuTest` (no colour literal in the menu's styles, rows and focus outline, roles and keys in
  the script, the tree button's attributes, the `design/admin` copies identical, a keyboard click told by its
  click count and not by its position on the page).

### Fixed
- On a scrolled page, a context menu opened with Enter, Space or Shift+F10 opened in the window's top left corner
  and the focus stayed on the button: `ezpopupmenu.js` took the click for a mouse click because its `pageX`/`pageY`
  carry the scroll offset. A click now counts as made with the keyboard by its click count (`detail` 0).

## 14 October 2026: landmarks and names in admin4

### Added
- admin4's frame has a skip link ("Skip to main content", the first stop of the Tab key, visible only while focused)
  and landmarks: the header as banner, the top menu, side menu and breadcrumb as named navigation, the right column
  as complementary, the footer as content info and `#maincontent` as main, also on the edit-type pages that bring
  their own `#maincontent`; a page without the left column whose template brings none (remove, copy, move ...) is
  wrapped in `#a4-main`, a main landmark without styles. The roles are on the existing elements, so no selector
  changes. Component
  `.a4-skip-link` in both copies of `components.css`. Guide: [the admin4 design: landmarks and names](../../features/6.0/admin4-design.md#landmarks-and-names).
- English and German texts for the skip link, the landmark names, the menu button and the new control names.

### Updated
- admin4: the breadcrumb and the top menu mark the current page (`aria-current`); disabled top menu entries are
  `aria-disabled` and out of the Tab order; the logo, "Open the site" and the phone-width menu button have names,
  and the menu button answers Enter and Space and reports `aria-expanded`; the section select of the Details tab,
  the sorting selects, the selection checkboxes and main language and location radios of the Translations and
  Locations tabs (with the row's language or location), the embed code fields of the relations list, the year,
  month and day fields of the date attribute and the disabled header search have names; the version preview frame
  has a title; the duplicate image link of the current user is hidden from screen readers and the Tab key.
- Every header cell of admin4's templates has `scope="col"`.
- Test: `AdminLandmarksTest`.

## 14 October 2026: keyboard use of admin4's tabs, content tree and date fields

### Updated
- admin4's node tabs follow the WAI-ARIA tabs pattern (tablist, tab with `aria-selected` and `aria-controls`,
  tabpanel): only the selected tab is in the Tab order (the first one when none is selected), the arrow keys, Home
  and End move between the tabs and open them. A click on an element inside a tab's link opens that tab (the handler took the link's parent of the clicked
  element). Guide: [the admin4 design: keyboard](../../features/6.0/admin4-design.md#keyboard).
- The show/hide tabs toggle is a button named "Show tabs" whose `aria-pressed` says whether the tabs are shown; its
  title says what a press does; Space presses it.
- The content tree's open/close link is a button named "Items below <node>" with `aria-expanded` (`aria-busy` while
  loading) that keeps the focus after a press; Space presses it. In the admin and admin4 designs (same script).
- The calendar of the date and date/time fields of admin4 opens from a button the keyboard reaches (the classic
  designs keep the image).
- English and German texts for the tree button, the calendar button and the tabs toggle. Test: `AdminKeyboardTest`.

## 14 October 2026: the content tree is a tree for screen readers and keyboards

### Updated
- The content tree of the admin and admin4 designs follows the WAI-ARIA tree view pattern: a `tree` named "Content
  tree", each node's name link a `treeitem` with level, position, `aria-expanded` and (current node)
  `aria-current="page"`, the sub items a `group` the item owns. One item is in the Tab order; Up/Down, Right
  (open, or first sub item), Left (close, or parent), Home, End and first-letter type-ahead move through the shown
  items, Enter follows the link, Shift+F10 or the context menu key opens the node's own menu. The open/close button
  and the node icon stay for the mouse, out of the Tab order and hidden from screen readers. A load error is read
  out through `aria-describedby`; a node whose sub items are all hidden has no `aria-expanded`.
  Guide: [the admin4 design: keyboard](../../features/6.0/admin4-design.md#keyboard).
- English and German text "Content tree". Test: `AdminTreeViewTest`.

## 14 October 2026: every form field of admin4 has a name

### Updated
- admin4: every `input`, `select` and `textarea` has an accessible name, about 230 fields in 76 templates that had
  only a title, a placeholder or their position: template creation, the toolbar editor, package export and
  creation, workflow events and the workflow editor, shop forms and lists (registration, currencies, VAT, discounts,
  order and product lists), the Setup > RAD wizards, RSS/OPML export, the version preview, relations, URL aliases,
  trash, browse lists and the remaining tables. Visible text names a field (`<label for>`, a label around the text
  of a radio or checkbox, the column header and the row's name, the card heading); radio groups are named groups.
  Fields that a title used to name keep the title and carry the same text as `aria-label`.
- New component `.a4-inline-label` in both copies of `components.css`: a label around text that was plain before,
  drawn like that text (`core.css` draws every label bold, larger and as a block).
- `design/standard/templates/shop/country/edit.tpl` takes an optional `select_id`, so a label can name the country
  select.

### Fixed
- The Cancel button of the remove-location confirmation (`node/removenode.tpl`) lost its closing quote.

### Added
- Test: `AdminFormLabelsTest`: no form field of admin4 without a name; every `<label for>` and `aria-labelledby`
  points at an id of its template (or one an include is given); no `aria-label` replaces a label that has text;
  the includes that take their id as a parameter (`select_id`) get it, with a label, from every admin4 template
  that includes them; and the checker's own cases.
- English and German texts for the five new field names.

## 14 October 2026: contrast of the admin4 colours

- The focus ring token `--a4-ring` is opaque: `#c2410c` light (5.2:1 on a card, 4.6:1 on the page), `#ff9a5c` dark.
  The translucent orange it replaces was 1.8:1 in light mode, below the 3:1 a focus indicator needs.
- The current item of the header menu is drawn in `--a4-accent-strong` (white text 5.2:1); `#f26a21` gave 3.1:1.
- Filled buttons keep 4.5:1 when hovered: the hover fill is `--a4-accent-press` and its text the new token
  `--a4-on-accent-hover` (white in light mode, dark ink on the light orange of dark mode). It replaces three
  dark-mode rules that borrowed `--a4-code-bg` as a text colour; the password wizard's primary button had none and
  was 2.3:1 in dark mode.
- Fields have one edge colour, `--a4-field-line`, raised to `#80879a` light and `#6f7a93` dark (3:1 on every surface);
  the fields of `#page` used `--a4-line-strong` (1.6:1).
- `--a4-chart-3`, `-4` and `-5` are darker in light mode (3:1 on a card).
- The header search placeholder, the current item of the left menu, the dashboard's version line and the focus
  outlines of the package pages take the tokens instead of five other oranges and a grey placeholder that was
  1.7:1 in dark mode.
- The Info pages (`ezinfo.css`), package compare (`packagecompare.css`), the extension order page
  (`setup-extensions.css`) and the package view take every colour from the tokens, so they follow the dark mode.
- A focused default button keeps the accent; `theme/rounded.css` painted it teal with yellow text.
- Status edges of package compare (changed rows, import entries, the import result) are drawn in the state colour
  itself (3:1), not its pale line colour; the package view's "ok" badge is the ok tint like the other pages; the
  community block's edge is the strong line colour.
- A just-moved bookmark or role policy is marked with the accent tint and edge instead of a second focus-like ring.
- Tests: `AdminTokenContrastTest` computes the contrast of the token pairs in both modes, each state's text on its
  own tint included (a translucent tint laid over the card); `AdminStylingRulesTest` checks `admin4.css` and the
  page stylesheets for colour literals.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 focus indicators

### Updated
- The header search's button, scope switch and the scope pane's close button show the focus ring again; their
  `:focus-visible` rules took the outline away and left a tint of about 1.1:1.
- The frame's fields that draw their focus as a box-shadow ring (the fields of `#page` outside `.a4-page`, the header
  search field, the light/dark switch, the sign-in fields and the password wizard's fields) set a transparent
  outline instead of none, so Windows high contrast (forced colors), which drops shadows, still shows the focus.
- The sidebar buttons' focus is the 3px ring of every other control (it was a 2px orange outline, 2.9:1 on the soft
  background).
- Test: `AdminStylingRulesTest` fails on a `:focus` rule of an admin4 stylesheet that removes the outline (`none`,
  `hidden` or width `0` in any order; `:focus:not(:focus-visible)` and `:focus-within` aside).

## 14 October 2026: admin4 and the modes the browser asks for

### Updated
- Each admin theme sets `color-scheme`, so scrollbars, select popups, date pickers and autofill are dark in dark mode.
- Forced colors (Windows high contrast): buttons, fields, cards, badges and chips get an edge, the current tab and
  selected rows and cards an outline in `Highlight`, a checked chip and the current step of a wizard a `Highlight`
  fill, and the data drawn as fills (status dots, meters, bar charts) keep their colours.
- `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` ends every transition and animation of admin4 at once (the sidebar slide, the
  dashboard's lift, the fades); the two `transition: all` rules name their properties.
- On touch screens the text fields, selects and text areas of admin4 use 16px text (date and time fields included),
  so a phone's browser does not zoom in when one gets the focus.
- A window narrower than 1024px no longer stores the sidebars as closed: there they open over the page one at a
  time, and a wide window shows them again as the user last left them there. Only a click on a wide window
  saves the choice, as the user's eZPreferences `admin_left_sidebar` and `admin_right_sidebar` (like the column
  width), which the page layout writes into `<body data-a4-sidebars>` and its cache key, so it follows the user
  from browser to browser. The browser storage keys used before (`ezpAdminLeftSidebar`, `ezpAdminRightSidebar`)
  held the "closed" every narrow visit had written and are removed, so both sidebars show again once. The width
  comes from `matchMedia`.
- The narrow padding of the content wrapper and the frame's narrow rules start below 1024px, not at 1024px too.
- Test: `AdminStylingRulesTest` checks the colour scheme, forced-colors, touch and reduced-motion rules (with the
  selectors that must win), that no stylesheet transitions `all`, and that the sidebar script stores only in one
  place, inside a `try`.

## 14 October 2026: one spacing and type scale for admin4

### Added
- Tokens `--a4-space-7` (48px), `--a4-text-2xl` (26px), `--a4-text-3xl` (32px) and `--a4-leading-tight` (1.25, for
  headings), in both copies of `components.css`.

### Updated
- `admin4.css` and `components.css` take every font size and spacing from the scales: about 30 pixel sizes
  between 11 and 32px and some 240 spacing lengths (3, 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 14, 18, 20, 22px …) are the nearest
  step now. No text of the frame is smaller than 12px (the dashboard's bar values and weekday labels and the node
  badges were 11 and 11.5px).
- Targets of at least 24 x 24px (WCAG 2.5.8): the sidebar tabs are 24px wide (the content column keeps a 24px
  gutter on both sides, so they cover no content), the search scope switch takes the click in a 24px area, the
  dialog close, the search scope pane's close and the page numbers are 24px. The content tree's open/close takes the
  click in a 20 x 20px area (more would cover the next row) and the row checkboxes are 18px; both rely on the
  spacing exception of 2.5.8 (rows about 27px apart). The light/dark switch and the search field are both 36px high.
- The password wizard keeps room for the wider show/hide button, and an invalid field's text does not move when its
  border grows to 2px. Its success heading is 16px again, below the page heading.
- Page numbers and the dashboard's bar labels wrap on a 360px phone instead of running out of their card.
- The node view's tabs on/off control (`a.show-hide-tabs`, `#maincontent-show` / `#maincontent-hide`) was a 16px icon
  in the gap above the tabs; it is a 36 x 24px switch (track and knob in the tokens, accent when on, forced-colors
  edges) with the visible label "Tabs" (new string in `design/admin/parts/my/menu`, ger-DE "Reiter"), at the right
  end of the tab row. It is styled by its class, ids and `aria-pressed`, so a button with `aria-pressed` in place of
  the link keeps the look.
- Test: `AdminStylingRulesTest` fails on a pixel font size or an off-scale spacing length in `admin4.css` or
  `components.css` (a few layout offsets listed per selector aside), and checks that the 24px targets stay.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 content editing says which fields are required and which failed

### Added
- Components `.a4-required-mark`, `.a4-required-hint` and `.a4-error-summary` in both copies of `components.css`;
  `.a4-bi-x-circle-fill`; the module classes `.a4-content-attr`, `.a4-content-attr-error` in `modules/content.css`.
  New string "Required field" in `design/admin/content/edit` (ger-DE "Pflichtfeld").
- Test: `AdminFormErrorsTest`.

### Updated
- Content editing in admin4 (`content/edit_attribute.tpl`, `edit_validation.tpl`; the attributes of edit, multiedit,
  user registration and the template look editor): each attribute is a named group (`role="group"`,
  `id="ezcca-attr-<attribute id>"`, `aria-labelledby` on its name; a grouped input keeps its fieldset and legend as
  name). A required one shows a red `*` with "required" for screen readers instead of the visible "(required)"; one
  hint above the attributes explains the mark, once per page and only when a field is required. An invalid one shows
  its message under the label (`.a4-field-error` with an icon, `id="ezcca-error-<id>"`, the description of the group or
  of the grouped input's fieldset) and a red edge. The page's script sets `aria-required`, `aria-invalid` and
  `aria-describedby` on the data controls the datatype templates draw, for every datatype at once: not on buttons,
  file or reset inputs, remove/selection checkboxes, the original side of a translation, or a control inside
  `.a4-no-field-mark` (the opt-out for datatype templates). The summary of a failed store is an alert that takes the
  focus on load, and its entries link to the fields; every attribute it names is marked invalid (also one that failed
  without a message of its own) and its collapsed group opens. Field names and posted values are unchanged.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 card parts

### Added
- The card component (`.a4-card`, both copies of components.css) has a foot, `.a4-card-foot`: the card's actions on
  a strip at its bottom edge; `.a4-card-actions` at the end of `.a4-card-head`; a `<p>` in the head is the subtitle.
  `.a4-card.is-flush` lets a table run to the card's edges; `.a4-card.is-link` makes the whole card follow the link in
  its title (focus ring around the card where the browser has `:has()`, the controls in body, foot and head actions
  stay clickable); `.a4-card.is-busy` with `aria-busy="true"` veils the card with a spinner while it loads (no motion
  when reduced motion is asked for; a script also sets `inert`). Foot and flush head take the card's padding from
  `--a4-card-pad-x`/`-y` when a density setting defines them, else from the spacing scale.

### Updated
- 16 cards that opened with a section head use the card's head and body: the maintenance page (3), RSS import (4) and
  export (3), the policy limitations (3, the node and subtree pickers with their buttons in the card's foot), the
  class view and edit, global URL aliases. Field names and posted values are unchanged.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 layout polish

### Added
- Token `--a4-control-h` (32px): the height of the controls that stand in a row. Token `--a4-zebra` (light `#f2f4f7`,
  dark `#212734`) for the even rows of data tables. In both copies of `components.css`.

### Updated
- Zebra rows in every data table: `table.list`, `.a4-table` and the sub-items table stripe every second data row with
  `--a4-zebra`, counting only rows with data cells (a header row of `th` in `tbody` or a `hidden` row is skipped, so
  the first data row is plain; a row a script hides still counts); a row header (`th scope="row"`) takes its row's
  colour. The hovered row takes `--a4-info-bg` (it was `--a4-soft`, the same as a stripe), a selected row keeps
  `--a4-accent-soft`. The `bglight`/`bgdark` row classes no longer set the colour; the RSS import's own unstriped
  list follows the others. Layout tables stay plain, and the VAT rule and currency forms, laid out as a `table.list`,
  take `a4-plain`. `--a4-accent-text` is `#bc3f0b` in light mode (was `#c2410c`): 4.7:1 on a hovered row too.
- The node view's language choice is as high as the buttons beside it (32px; Chrome kept a native select at 19px):
  drawn by the page with the button edge and radius and a chevron in the tokens; forced colors keep the native control.
- The sub-items toolbar (Select, Create new, Create multiple, More actions, Table options) and the pager buttons draw
  like the node's action buttons: 32px high, the button radius and edge, ink text in weight 600, the same hover, the
  global 3px focus ring (the toolbar was 24px with 3px corners and orange text, the pager 27 x 33px). The current
  page number is the accent tint.
- The side columns: no rule above the first heading of a card, which starts at the card's top (the right column's
  first heading sat 19px down, now 8px). The content tree's rows are 24px apart at the small and medium column sizes
  (a 20px line and 4px), the smallest spacing WCAG 2.5.8 allows for its links; the large size gets a 1.25em line. The
  connector elbow and the open/close symbol sit on the line's middle. In forced colors the language choice is the
  native control without the page's chevron.
- The left column's width grip was a grey gradient bar; it is a small card on the column's edge with two rows of dots,
  the resize cursor and the accent on hover, drag and focus. It is a focusable separator now (`role="separator"`,
  `aria-valuenow` in pixels): the arrow keys change the width by 16px (Shift: 64px), Home and End go to the narrowest
  and widest (the widest follows the window), the width is saved when the key is let go. It is named by its
  `aria-label` "Width of the left column" (new string in `design/admin/pagelayout`, ger-DE, eng-US).
- Tests: `AdminStylingRulesTest` checks the shared zebra and that no admin4 stylesheet paints rows in a colour of its
  own, the controls' common height and the grip; `AdminTokenContrastTest` checks text on `--a4-zebra`.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 lists and the path in the content tree

### Updated
- Data tables (`table.list`, `.a4-table`, the sub-items table): cells sit in the middle of their row (`.a4-plain`
  form tables keep their labels at the top); a row whose selection checkbox is ticked takes the selection tint
  without a script.
- Empty lists say so in the `.a4-empty` component (28 templates: roles and policies, shop lists and currencies,
  trash, content jobs, relations, states, workflows, template overrides).
- The content tree shows where the page is: the current node's name in the accent tint with a straight bar on its
  left (square left corners; a Highlight edge in forced colors), the nodes above it in a stronger weight. CSS only,
  from the existing `currentnode` class and `:has()`.
- The choices above a list (`p.table-preferences`: 10 | 25 | 50 items, the shop archive's sort field and order, the
  filters of Setup > RAD) are a segmented control on the tokens: surface background, `--a4-line` edges, links in the
  accent text colour, the current choice in the accent tint with ink text in weight 650, targets of at least 28px,
  Highlight for the current choice in forced colors. Before, `theme/rounded.css` (admin4's copy only) drew them as a
  light grey gradient with fixed colours, a light box in dark mode whose current value could not be read.
- Tests: `AdminStylingRulesTest::testListsMarkTickedRows`, `testTreeShowsThePathToTheCurrentNode`,
  `testEmptyListsUseTheEmptyState`, `testTablePreferencesAreASegmentedControlOnTheTokens`.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 stacking layers and the side column tabs

### Added
- One scale of stacking tokens `--a4-z-*` in both copies of `components.css` (raised, sticky, frame-grip,
  frame-control, hover-preview, popover, scroll-top, navbar, sidebar, sidebar-handle, controlbar, drag-target, header,
  sidebar-toggle, menu, menu-sub, overlay, modal, toast, drag, drag-mask). All 29 z-index numbers of admin4's
  stylesheets take one of them, in the order they had; a number is left only for 0, 1 and -1 inside one component.
  The duplicate, dead `div.popupmenu#EditSubmenu` rule is gone; the base `#overlay-mask` rule of
  `theme/modalwindow.css` takes `--a4-z-overlay` too. Guide: [Styling the administration](../../bc/6.0/admin4-styling.md#stacking-layers).

### Updated
- Context menus lie above the fixed header (`--a4-z-menu` 1040, the edit submenu 1050; they were 1010 and 1020), so a
  menu that opens upwards is not cut off.
- A dialog's dimming mask and the dialog (`#overlay-mask`, `.modal-window`) lie above the header, the side column tabs
  and menus (`--a4-z-overlay` 1060, `--a4-z-modal` 1070; they were 121 and 122, under the header).
- The tabs on the window edges that show and hide the side columns are named after their column ("Left column",
  "Right column"), say with `aria-expanded` whether it shows and with `aria-controls` which it is; on a narrow window
  Escape closes a column open over the page and gives the focus back to its tab. New strings in
  `design/admin/pagelayout`.
- Tests: `AdminStylingRulesTest::testStackingComesFromTheLayerTokens` (no other z-index, the order of the layers),
  `testSidebarTabsSayWhatTheyControl`.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 page header and breadcrumb

### Added
- Components `.a4-pagehead` (pretitle, title, subtitle or meta line, actions on the right that wrap under the title
  on narrow windows) and `ol.a4-crumbs` (the breadcrumb), in both copies of `components.css`.

### Updated
- The breadcrumb (`page_toppath.tpl`) is an ordered list in the `#path` navigation landmark ("Breadcrumb", new string
  in `design/admin/pagelayout`, ger-DE, eng-US): the separators come from the stylesheet and are not read out, the
  current step carries `aria-current="page"`, "You are here:" is read by screen readers only, `role="list"` keeps the
  list semantics in Safari. Names are no longer cut to 18 characters: a long name ends in an ellipsis (28 characters,
  the current page 48) and keeps its full name as title (from 20 characters on, as the cut is by width). On windows up to 600px the middle steps fold into one "..."
  step for the eye only: they stay readable for screen readers and come back when they take the keyboard focus. An
  empty path renders nothing. The old `#path span` colour, which beat the breadcrumb's own colours, is gone.
- One title style for every page: the kernel's `h1.context-title`, the node view's title and the dashboard's greeting
  take `--a4-text-xl` (22px, weight 650; they were 1.35em, 1.45em and 26px), `--a4-text-l` (16px) on windows up to
  600px, one breakpoint for all three. The
  node view's header row is built from `.a4-pagehead`.
- Test: `AdminPageHeaderTest` (the components in both stylesheets, the breadcrumb's list, current step and full names,
  the landmark, the one title size).

## 14 October 2026: admin4 notices with icons, and toasts

### Added
- Toasts: `window.a4Toast( message, kind )` in `admin4.js` shows a short feedback of an action done in the page in one
  of two live regions of the frame (`#a4-toasts`, polite; `#a4-toasts-alert`, assertive, for `kind` `bad`). A toast
  does not take the focus, closes after 5 s, waits while the pointer or the focus is on it, and has a close button;
  without motion when the system asks for reduced motion. Components `.a4-toasts`, `.a4-toast` in both copies of
  `components.css`. Used by the node view's copy chips (node, object and remote id) and the menu configuration's
  copy buttons, whose tick was only seen.
- `.a4-notice-title` (a bold first line) and `.no-icon` for `.a4-notice`.
- New strings: "Dismiss" (design/admin/pagelayout), "Copied to the clipboard" (design/admin/node/view/full,
  design/admin/visual/menuconfig), in ger-DE and eng-US.

### Updated
- `.a4-notice.is-ok`, `.is-bad`, `.is-warn` and `.is-info` show their state by an icon too, not by colour alone
  (WCAG 1.4.1): check, cross, triangle and `i` in the state's colour left of the text; in forced colors in the text
  colour. New icon `info-circle-fill` in `share/icons/bootstrap-icons/svg/`.
- The classic `div.message-*` that modules render after an action look like `.a4-notice` (a state tint and edge,
  the heading's icon in the state's colour) instead of a 6px black or coloured frame, and carry a role: 25
  `message-error` are `role="alert"`, the 48 warnings, feedbacks and confirmations `role="status"`. A status region
  that is already in the page is usually not announced when the page loads, so a warning about a standing state
  (audit switched off) does not interrupt; screen readers differ here. A message without a heading takes the icon
  on the block itself.
- The setup wizards' notices lose their hand-written "!" and tick, which stood next to the icon now; a tick notice is
  `is-ok`.
- A copy in the node view or the menu configuration that fails says so ("Could not copy", a toast for errors), and
  the textarea fallback gives the focus back to the chip. Toasts with the same text replace each other, at most three
  stay; a toast closed with its button gives the focus back to the element the action came from. `li.a4-notice` has
  no list marker.
- Test: `AdminNoticesTest` (an icon for every state, the forced-colors rule, the toast component and live regions,
  the toast writes text only and never moves the focus, every classic message has its role, every copy chip names
  its toast).

## 14 October 2026: admin4 follows the system's light or dark mode on request

### Updated
- The header's mode switch (admin4, admin4l) steps light -> dark -> system. "System" follows `prefers-color-scheme`,
  also when the operating system changes it while the page is open. The icon (sun, moon, new half circle
  `.a4-bi-circle-half` from Bootstrap Icons 1.13.1) and the button's accessible name say the mode in use ("Colour
  mode: light", "… dark", "… system"); the title names the next step ("Switch to dark mode"), and a status region
  beside the switch announces a press. The button no longer carries `aria-pressed`, which can only say two states.
  admin4l's switch draws its sun and moon as admin icons too (they were inline SVGs in black, lost in dark mode and
  forced colours). Other open admin tabs follow the change. Without a stored
  choice the admin stays light, as before; "system" as the default would be a separate decision. The stylesheets keep
  reading `html[data-a4-theme]` (light or dark); `html[data-a4-theme-choice]` is new. An older copy of the switch
  without `data-label-system` keeps stepping between light and dark. New strings "Color mode: light/dark/system"
  and "Switch to light/dark/system mode" in design/admin/pagelayout (ger-DE "Farbmodus: …", "Zum … Modus wechseln",
  eng-US). Test: `AdminThemeSwitchTest`.

## 14 October 2026: a timeline for admin4's audit events

### Added
- The component `.a4-timeline` (both copies of components.css): an ordered list, newest first, one event per item
  with its time (`<time datetime>`, the UTC instant), the event, its meta and a state badge; a dot on a line marks
  each event in the state's colour (`.is-ok`, `.is-warn`, `.is-bad`, `.is-info`), which the badge also says in
  words. Two columns, one on narrow windows; the line is an edge and the dots keep their colour in forced colors.
  For short chronological lists without actions per row; a list with checkboxes or buttons per row stays a table.
- `design/admin4/templates/parts/audit_timeline.tpl` (`events`, optional `show_reason`): audit events as a timeline,
  actor and result named for screen readers.

### Updated
- admin4 draws the dashboard's audit block (`dashboard/audit.tpl`) and the node view's Audit tab
  (`tabs/audit/node.tpl`) as a timeline instead of a four-column table; the fetches and links are unchanged, the
  other designs keep their tables. The full event list (`audit/recent`, six columns) and the version history (a
  removal checkbox per row) stay tables.
- Test: `AdminTimelineTest` (the component in both stylesheets, the dots per state, the line as an edge and the
  forced-colors rule; the part as an ordered list with `datetime`, washed values and named actor and result; the two
  audit lists use the part).

## 14 October 2026: one release of each jQuery library in ezjscore

### Removed
- ezjscore no longer ships the older jQuery copies that nothing loaded: `jquery-3.7.1.js`,
  `jquery-3.7.1.min.js`, `jquery-migrate-3.4.1.min.js` and `jquery-ui-1.10.3.custom.min.js` (jQuery UI 1.10.3 has
  known vulnerabilities, among them CVE-2016-7103, CVE-2021-41182, CVE-2021-41183 and CVE-2021-41184). No template,
  setting or script of the distribution named them; `ezjsc::jquery` and `ezjsc::jqueryUI` load jQuery 4.0.0,
  Migrate 4.0.2 and jQuery UI 1.14.2 as before.
- Test: `ezjscoreVendoredLibrariesTest` (one release of each library in the folder, every `LocalScripts[]` file of
  ezjscore.ini exists, the CDN entries and extension.xml name the shipped release; mutation-checked; and no other
  extension's, siteaccess's or override's `ezjscore.ini` names a jQuery file that no design holds).
- ezjscore 1.7.0 (the kernel copy and the standalone package) carries this change; sevenx_themes_simple 1.0.22 no
  longer sets the older files for its siteaccesses.

### Upgrade notes
- A template or extension of your own that names one of the removed files directly (for example
  `{ezscript_require( 'jquery-3.7.1.min.js' )}`) gets no script any more. Use `ezjsc::jquery` or `ezjsc::jqueryUI`
  instead.
- An `ezjscore.ini` of your own (in `settings/override`, a siteaccess or an extension) that sets
  `LocalScripts[jquery]`, `[jqueryMigrate]` or `[jqueryUI]` to one of the removed files loads nothing for that key:
  remove the line, so ezjscore's default applies, or name `jquery-4.0.0.min.js`, `jquery-migrate-4.0.2.min.js` or
  `jquery-ui-1.14.2.min.js`.

## 14 October 2026: normal or compact density in admin4

### Added
- A density switch in admin4's header beside the light/dark button: a button named "Compact view" whose
  `aria-pressed` says whether the compact density is on (new strings "Compact view" and "Switch between normal and
  compact view" in design/admin/pagelayout, ger-DE, eng-US). The choice is kept in the browser (`localStorage`
  "exp-admin4-density", guarded for blocked storage) and applied before the page is drawn, like the mode.
- Density tokens in both copies of components.css: `--a4-list-pad` (`table.list` cells, `.4em` as before),
  `--a4-cell-pad-y`/`-x` (`.a4-table`), `--a4-card-pad-y`/`-x` (`.a4-card`); `html[data-a4-density="compact"]` sets
  only them and `--a4-control-h` anew (`.25em`, 4/8 px, 8/12 px, 28 px). Every target keeps 24 px (WCAG 2.5.8); the
  content tree keeps its row pitch. The normal density looks as before.
- Test: `AdminDensityTest` (the compact block sets only density tokens, compact controls stay at 24 px or more, the
  tree is not tightened, tables and cards read the tokens, the switch is a pressed button applied in the head script,
  the texts are translated; mutation-checked).

## 14 October 2026: the admin4 dashboard's figures

### Updated
- The key figures of the admin4 dashboard (`content/dashboard.tpl`) have an icon tile each (Bootstrap Icons 1.13.1:
  new `graph-up-arrow`, `person-check`, `arrow-up-right`, `arrow-down-right`, `dash` and their `.a4-bi-*` classes),
  and a figure that is a link shows an arrow in its corner. Tiles are at least 180px wide, one column on a phone.
- "Published in the last 7 days" says how its full days compare with the 6 days before them: `+5 against the 6 days before, without today` (today is not over, so the trend leaves it out), with an
  arrow, in the muted text colour (a rise is not always good). Both weeks are summed from the 14 days the chart
  already counts, so the dashboard runs one query fewer than before.
- The chart's numbers fold out under it as a table ("Show the numbers"), so the data is there in words for everyone,
  not only as bar heights.
- New strings in design/admin/dashboard (ger-DE, eng-US). Test: `AdminDashboardTest`.

## 14 October 2026: admin4 left menu groups

### Added
- menu.ini can group a long left menu: `Groups[]` (order), `GroupNames[<group>]` (title), `GroupLinks_<group>[]`
  (link keys). admin4's `parts/ini_menu.tpl` draws each group as a `<details>` with a small-capital title
  (`summary.a4-nav-title`, a caret that turns), open unless the user closed it (`admin4.js` keeps the choice per
  browser in localStorage) and always open when it holds the current page; links in no group follow under "More".
  Leftmenu_setup is grouped into Content model, Site and URLs, Workflows, Users and messages and System. Without
  `Groups[]`, or with `Groups[]` reset to no values, a menu is one list, as before; a link named in two groups shows
  in the first; the access checks per link are unchanged.
- The current page's link in a left menu carries `aria-current="page"` and takes the accent tint with a straight bar on
  its left (square left corners; a Highlight edge in forced colors); a plain menu's list is named by its heading (`aria-labelledby`). The
  links are drawn by the new include `parts/ini_menu_items.tpl`.
- The left column is a navigation landmark named "Side menu" (`page_leftmenu.tpl`).
- New strings: "Side menu" (design/admin/pagelayout), the group titles and "More" (design/admin/parts/setup/menu),
  ger-DE and eng-US.
- Test: `AdminLeftMenuGroupsTest` (every setup link in exactly one group, every group named, the details markup and
  the flat fallback, the unchanged access checks, aria-current and the current link's square left corners, the
  landmark, the stored state never closing the
  current page's group).

## 14 October 2026: each admin links to its own site

### Added
- `site.ini [SiteSettings] FrontSiteAccess`: the site an admin siteaccess belongs to. Its "Open the site" link
  (admin3, admin4, admin4l) and the node view's "View on site" go there; empty (the default) keeps
  `DefaultAccess`. Before, every admin of an installation that serves several sites from one tree linked to the
  same default site. Set it in the admin siteaccess's `site.ini.append.php`. The template operator
  `siteaccess_url()` without a parameter now calls the new `ezpSiteAccessURL::front()`; with a parameter, and
  `ezpSiteAccessURL::root()`, nothing changes. A siteaccess that is not in `AvailableSiteAccessList` falls back to
  `DefaultAccess` with a debug warning. The header cache blocks key on the address, so each admin caches its own
  link. Compiled templates still call the old code until the template cache is cleared: after updating, clear the
  caches of each admin siteaccess (`php bin/php/ezcache.php --clear-all -s <admin siteaccess>`). Tests:
  `ezpSiteAccessURLTest` (`front()` without, with and with an unknown `FrontSiteAccess`, padded value),
  `eZKernelTemplateOperatorsTest` (the compiled template calls `front()`).

## 14 October 2026: the admin4 accessibility series, merged and refined

The 24 changes above (icons, language badges, context menus, landmarks, keyboard, tree view, form names, contrast,
focus, modes, scale, tables, lists, layers, form errors, page header, notices, mode switch, timeline, density,
dashboard, cards, menu groups, front site access) were merged together and refined:

### Added
- `window.a4Pref` (the first script of `page_head_style.tpl`) and `design:parts/a4_preference_attributes.tpl`: the
  frame's remembered choices are the signed-in user's eZPreferences, written on `<html>` before the page is drawn:
  `admin_color_mode` (light, dark, system), `admin_density` (normal, compact), `admin_left_sidebar` and
  `admin_right_sidebar` (the columns on a wide window), `admin_navgroups` (closed left menu groups). An anonymous
  page keeps them in the browser only; a choice the browser held before is taken along once. Test:
  `AdminPreferencesTest`.
- `window.a4CardBusy( card, on, message )` (admin4.js): a busy card sets `aria-busy` and `inert` together and
  announces a short message in a polite live region.
- Layer tokens `--a4-z-skip-link`, `--a4-z-controlbar-top`, `--a4-z-card-veil`, `--a4-z-card-spinner`; the frame
  tokens `--a4-explayouts-nav`, `--a4-explayouts-nav-edge` and `--a4-pw-toggle-room` (each with its dark value).

### Updated
- admin4l (the admin siteaccess): its page layout has the skip link, the landmarks, the named side and top menus,
  the width grip as a focusable separator and the toast regions of admin4; its header blocks are copies of admin4's
  top menu again (Bootstrap icons, column tabs, mode and density switches), and `AdminIconsTest` keeps them in step.
- No text on an admin4 page is below 12px: `core.css` sets the body to `--a4-text-s` (13px) and every em size of
  the base and page stylesheets that would land below 12px takes `--a4-text-xs`. The styling test keeps no color
  or spacing exception list any more.
- Tables: the zebra's row filter sits in `:where()`, so a hovered or selected row shows its state; compact list
  rows keep 24px targets.
- Context menus: Enter runs an item without a link too, a letter moves to the next item starting with it, the title
  is no item of the menu, the tree's menu buttons are named "Actions for <node>", and the class and node icon menus
  carry `aria-haspopup` before their first use.
- The content tree's buttons name a node as it is called (no doubled entities for umlauts or `&`), its items name
  Shift+F10 in `aria-keyshortcuts`; the node tabs move with Arrow Left and Right only; each calendar button is named
  after its field.
- Language badge links: the accessible name starts with the code shown (WCAG 2.5.3).
- Contrast: the current and just-moved row bars, the header search edge, the language select edge and the tabs
  switch's on edge reach 3:1; mask icons and status dots stay visible in forced colors; reduced motion also stops
  jQuery's effects.
- Side column tabs name their column, report `aria-expanded`, and on a page without that column name no target;
  the narrow and wide rules meet at 1023.98px.
- Content editing: every validation message is escaped exactly once in the summary and at the field, and a summary
  link scrolls its field below the fixed header. A warning that reports a failed form is `role="alert"`.
- Toasts lie on `--a4-z-toast` above dialogs and above the debug bar; an error toast stays until dismissed; Escape
  closes a focused toast.
- Breadcrumb: the last step is the current page also while editing; a name over 20 characters keeps its full name
  as title.
- Dashboard: the trend compares full days only ("against the 6 days before, without today"); the bar chart leaves
  its numbers to the table for screen readers.
- Audit timeline results, the left menu's "More" group and the mode switch ("Color mode") are translated; the
  left menu marks only the page itself as `aria-current="page"`.
- `ezpSiteAccessURL::front()` warns about an unknown `FrontSiteAccess` once per request also on Velocity's
  persistent workers.

### Upgrade notes
- Clear the template, template-override, template-block and content caches after the update (new templates and
  stylesheets), and hard-reload a browser that still shows the old stylesheet.

## 14 October 2026: admin4's base stylesheets on the tokens

### Fixed
- Links of the base stylesheets (`core.css` `a`, `ul.tabs`, the datatable's editable cells) take `--a4-accent-text`:
  the old `#0b4e7a` was 1.8:1 on a dark card (now 7.7:1; 5.5:1 in light mode).
- The diff marks of `content.css` are drawn from the tokens everywhere, as the history page already was: added text
  `--a4-ok`, removed text `--a4-bad`, the shown side's changes ink on `--a4-warn-bg` (light text on yellow was 1.1:1
  in dark mode, now 10.5:1), block changes on the ok and bad tints.
- The focused header search field kept a pale yellow background under light text in dark mode (1.1:1); it is the
  field colour. The search button's hover is the header's own, not a teal border.
- A focused `input.button` looks like a hovered one (`theme/rounded.css` painted it `rgba(0, 0, 0, .1)` over
  admin4.css); the ring marks the keyboard focus.
- `.a4-meter` fills with `--a4-accent-text`: 5.1:1 (light) and 7.0:1 (dark) on its track, where `--a4-accent` was
  2.9:1 in light mode (both copies of components.css).
- The context menu's focus ring is `var(--a4-ring)` in both modes, without a dark-mode rule of its own.
- The search scope pane fits a phone (`min(300px, 100vw - 32px)`); its title line takes `--a4-line`.
- The tabs on the window edges that show the side columns take `--a4-field-line` with the card colour on it (3.2:1
  and 4.0:1 on the page, the arrow 3.6:1 and 3.8:1 on the tab), instead of a fixed grey.
- In forced colors (Windows high contrast) every module state shown only by border, background or shadow (a current
  chip or tab, a selected or moved card or row, a pressed preset, a changed settings bar) gets a Highlight outline
  (`modules/*.css`, both copies).

### Added
- A print style for admin4: the content alone on the full width, no header, menus, side columns, control bars,
  toasts or footer, no shadows; the head script prints in the light colours and goes back to the chosen mode
  afterwards. No dark fallback without JavaScript: the admin stays light then, its documented default.
- Tests: `AdminStylingRulesTest::testBaseStylesheetsUseOnlyColourTokens` (no colour literal in core.css, content.css,
  pagelayout.css and the three theme stylesheets; the allowlist for kept literals is empty and only shrinks),
  `AdminBaseStylesheetsTest` (links, diff marks, forced-colors outlines, meter, button focus, menu ring, header
  parts, removed files, no vendor-prefixed leftovers, print) and the meter pairs in `AdminTokenContrastTest`; each
  new assertion was mutation-checked.

### Updated
- The rest of the colour literals of the base stylesheets are tokens as well (messages, rulers, the datatable and
  modal window headers, the default button's base colours, the footer links, the phone menu); the ones admin4.css
  overrode keep looking the same.

### Removed
- `design/admin4/stylesheets/responsive.css`: all but one rule was commented out. That rule (the padding of the
  status messages) is in `pagelayout.css`; `BackendCSSFileList[]=responsive.css` is gone from `settings/design.ini`,
  and admin3 names its own `responsive.css` in `page_head_style.tpl`, so admin3 looks the same.
- `setup-extensions.css` in admin4 and admin: no kernel template loaded it. The Admin UI design (exp_adminui) loaded
  it through its design fallback and ships its own copy from exp_adminui 1.0.0.17 on.
- Rules of admin4's base stylesheets for markup no admin4 template writes: the admin3 login page (`.loginpage`,
  `.login-input-wrapper`, `#icon-login` and the old `#logintext`/`#loginbutton` look, which admin4's sign-in page
  replaced), `#header-topmenu`, `#header-usermenu`, `#canvas-top`/`-bottom`, `p.versions`; and `-moz-`, `-ms-`, `-o-`
  transitions, shadows and gradients, `filter: alpha()`, `zoom: 1` and the invalid `linear-gradient(top, …)`.

### Upgrade notes
- A site whose own `design.ini` override still lists `BackendCSSFileList[]=responsive.css` keeps working: admin4
  then finds admin3's identical file through the design fallback.
- An extension that styled admin3's login page through admin4's `pagelayout.css` (`.login-input-wrapper` ...) needs
  its own rules; admin4's sign-in page never used them.

## 14 October 2026: names and request values in the admin designs are inserted as text

### Fixed
- Node, class, section and language names in the content tree menu, the popup menus and the relations list are
  inserted as text, and the sub-items name filter is escaped. The content tree keeps each node's menu data in the
  script and its menu button carries only `data-node-id`; one click listener opens the menu (mouse, Enter, Space,
  Shift+F10 and the context menu key as before). `ezpopmenu_showTopLevel()` keeps its parameters; its
  `menuHeader` is now set as plain text, and the `%...%` values of a menu item's `content` go into the item as
  text and attribute values (in all three copies of ezpopupmenu.js). `ezpopmenu_submitForm()` also fills the
  placeholders of the form's `action`.
- admin, admin3, admin4 (and standard where the same line exists): request values and stored texts that were
  printed raw are escaped for where they stand (the login form's redirect address, the template list filter, the
  shop list offset, URL and workflow fields, currency and package error texts, validation messages, relation
  attribute and translation names, left menu names); names passed to a popup menu from an `onclick` attribute are
  escaped for the script and then for the attribute. The link class preview links only to http(s), mailto and
  addresses without a scheme and shows anything else as text.
- `wash( 'javascript' )` also escapes `<`, `>` and `&` (as `\x3C`, `\x3E`, `\x26`), line breaks (`\n`, `\r`) and the
  line and paragraph separators, so its output is safe inside a script element and inside an HTML attribute as well as
  in a JavaScript string; the value a script reads is unchanged. A JS-escaped value in an HTML attribute (an `onclick`) is HTML-escaped as well
  (`|wash(javascript)|wash`). The sub-items name filter reaches the server URL-encoded and as typed.
- After the confirmation, `content/hide` returns to the page the user came from: the view has the `administration`
  UI context, so its confirmation page is not stored as the page to return to.
- `content/hide` hides or reveals a node only on a POST; a link (GET) shows the confirmation form, in English and
  German. The Advanced popup menu's "Hide / unhide" and the Hide and Reveal buttons of the Locations tab (admin,
  admin4; new shared class `.a4-link-button`) submit a form.
- The content tree icon's title is escaped once (a translation with an apostrophe showed `&#039;`). The redirect
  page of `DebugRedirection` escapes its address.
- Tests: `AdminTreeMenuEscapingTest` (also: no kernel design links to `content/hide`), `eZTemplateOperatorMatrixTest`
  (wash javascript in a script and with `&`).

### Upgrade notes
- A custom template or extension that links to `content/hide/<node>` now gets the confirmation page first; post a
  form to the same address to act at once. A `menuHeader` passed to `ezpopmenu_showTopLevel()` with markup in it
  shows the markup as text. Output of `wash( 'javascript' )` used outside JavaScript now shows `\x3C` for `<` and `\x26` for `&`. Without
  ezformtoken, `content/hide` is protected by the POST and the browser's SameSite default only; ezformtoken adds the
  form token.
  Clear the template and template-override caches after the update: compiled templates keep the old escaping
  until then.

## 14 October 2026: the search of the administration

### Fixed
- content/search and content/advancedsearch take the section, the class, the class attribute, the period, the
  subtrees and the time limit of the request as integers; anything that is no positive number is none. The ezjscore
  search (`ezjscServerFunctionsJs::search()`) passes only positive integer ids (`makePostIDArray()`) and an offset
  and limit of at least 0 and 1. A class may still be named by its identifier (`SearchContentClassID=article`,
  `SearchContentClassIdentifier`): both searches look it up to its id (`makePostClassIDArray()`,
  `AdvancedSearch::classID()`), and an identifier that names no class is no class filter. Before, the values reached the search engine as they were sent, and eZ Find wrote
  them into its filter queries. Updating eZ Find as well is recommended: its query builder now casts the ids itself.
- A section that does not exist no longer stops content/search and content/advancedsearch with a fatal error: the
  search is not limited to a section then.
- content/advancedsearch: a class attribute that does not exist, or that belongs to another class than the one
  searched, is no attribute filter (before: an error with eZ Find, or no result at all). The page opened without
  anything to search for runs no search; before it listed every object.
- A negative offset is 0; a search text is cut to `site.ini [SearchSettings] MaximumSearchTextWords` words (16) and
  `MaximumSearchTextLength` characters (1024), with a note on the page. Every word is a clause of the search
  engine's query, and a very long text made the search fail.
- The pages of a search keep where it searches: every subtree as `SubTreeArray[]` and the section (before: the
  subtrees joined by commas, which the view dropped, and no section); also in the browse mode. The advanced
  search's pages pass on the words as typed, not with the phrase in front of them a second time.
- The title of content/advancedsearch is escaped, as content/search's already was.
- The built-in search engine (`eZSearchEngine`, without eZ Find) writes the class attribute and the subtrees into
  its SQL as integers (`eZSearchEngine::subTreeNodeIDList()`); a subtree id that was not above 0 no longer leaves a
  stray comma in the node query.

### Updated
- admin4, content/search and content/advancedsearch: a search the engine could not run shows an alert
  (`.a4-notice.is-bad`, `role="alert"`) instead of "No results" and search tips; an empty text shows a prompt
  instead of "Search for <>"; the search form is a search landmark with a visible label and a search field. Each
  result shows the path of its location, so objects of the same name can be told apart.
- admin4 pager (`navigator/google.tpl`): a navigation landmark "Pages", the current page marked with
  `aria-current="page"`.
- admin4 header search: the sections of its popup are fetched once for both copies (`page_topmenu.tpl`), the name of
  the node searched in comes from the page's path when it is on it, and the browse mode's page size never exceeds
